**Verdict: not a clean green yet.** The code itself is in good shape and I would merge it. Two non-code items in the PR need your decision first, and I think both should come out before merge.

I read all 118 files on the branch, every commit, the CI runs and the repo activity log. The requirements file on main is a product spec, and this PR is a scaffold that does not implement any FR yet, which the PR body states plainly.

**Security scan: clean.** No obfuscated code, no long-line payloads, no eval or child_process in configs, no blockchain URLs, no secrets, no force pushes. The `cn` dependency looked odd but it is shadcn's own package on npm. All three CI checks passed.

**Blocking items**

- **A full Claude Code transcript is committed** under `.claude-logs/`, and CLAUDE.md instructs every future session to commit these logs. The committed one holds no keys, only Pratyush's name, GitHub login, local paths and the Supabase project ref. The mechanism is the problem: the hook script has no redaction, so any key pasted into a prompt later goes straight to GitHub. Ask Pratyush to drop the log, gitignore the folder and remove the "always commit" rule.
- **The repo ships hooks that run on your Mac.** A `.claude/settings.json` runs a Python script on every prompt and every stop. It reads your local Claude transcripts and writes them into the repo. Husky also installs git hooks on `pnpm install`. I read the whole script and it is benign: it runs `git config` and `gh api user`, nothing else leaves the machine. But under your standing rules you should accept these knowingly, not by default. Note the hook records whichever `gh` account is active, so it would expose your two-account setup if it ever logged a [private]-adjacent session.

**Should fix, non-blocking**

- Sign-up passes Supabase's raw error message to the user. That contradicts the PR's own convention and allows account enumeration.
- The page-level `requireUser` redirect drops the return path that the proxy adds.
- The logger reads `process.env` directly, breaking the PR's own env rule.
- The shadcn CLI sits in runtime dependencies. GitHub Actions use major tags, and the Supabase CLI action uses `latest`.
- The profiles migration was already pushed to the hosted project before review. Low impact here, but worth a word with Pratyush about process.
- The Semantic PR check has not run yet. That is expected, since it only activates once the workflow is on main.

**Product question for you, not Pratyush:** the scaffold assumes children aged 11 to 14 sign up with their own email and password and give a full name. The requirements never say whether the child or a parent holds the account, and under-13 consent rules apply either way. Worth settling before the next feature lands.

Nothing here is posted to GitHub yet. If you want, I can turn the blocking and should-fix items into a review on the PR.

Understood, and I confirmed the scoping claim against the script rather than taking it on trust.

**Green signal. Merge it.**

The hook is scoped to Jarso by construction. The settings file lives in this repo, so Claude Code only fires it when Jarso is the project directory. The script then reads only the transcript folder for that same directory and writes only into this repo's `.claude-logs/`. It cannot touch [private] or any other project's sessions. Two small caveats on how it behaves, neither of which blocks:

- It picks the most recently modified transcript in the Jarso folder, not the current session's. If two Jarso sessions run at the same time, one can log the other's turns.
- It has no redaction, which you already know. Treat anything pasted into a Jarso prompt as published.

With the logging accepted as intentional, nothing in the PR blocks. The code is sound, the migration and RLS are correct, the security scan was clean, CI is green, and there were no force pushes.

The should-fix items stand as follow-ups, not merge conditions:

- Sign-up passes the raw Supabase error message to the user, which allows account enumeration.
- The page-level sign-in redirect drops the return path the proxy adds.
- The logger reads `process.env` directly against the PR's own env rule.
- The shadcn CLI is in runtime dependencies, and the Supabase CLI action is unpinned.

The product question about who holds the account for a child under 13 is still open and worth settling before the first real feature lands.

I have not posted anything to GitHub. Say the word and I will approve the PR there with the follow-ups listed as comments.

All five fixes are on main and CI is green on the result.

**What landed** (five commits, pushed directly to main as harisshakeel, each with your attribution line):

- **Sign-up no longer shows Supabase's raw error text.** The form now shows a generic message and the error code goes to the server log, which closes the account-enumeration gap and matches the repo's own no-raw-errors rule.
- **Page-level auth redirects keep the return path.** The `requireUser` helper now takes the page path and sends signed-out users to login with it, matching what the proxy already did. The dashboard passes its route, and docs/auth.md says to do the same for new pages.
- **The logger validates `LOG_LEVEL`** instead of casting it blindly, so a typo falls back to info rather than logging everything. CLAUDE.md and the conventions doc now name the logger as the one allowed direct env read, since it runs in the browser too.
- **The shadcn CLI moved to devDependencies**, in both package.json and the lockfile.
- **The Supabase CLI in CI is pinned** to the same version the repo depends on, with a comment to keep them in sync.

**Verification.** I could not run pnpm locally under your standing rules, so the lockfile edit was done by hand. The CI run on the pushed commit confirms it: install with frozen lockfile, lint, typecheck, unit tests, build, three Playwright smoke tests and the migration reset all passed.

**Two things I left alone.** GitHub Actions are still pinned to major tags rather than commit SHAs. And no session log was added for my work, because the repo's logging hook was not active in this session. CI printed deprecation warnings that the checkout, setup-node and pnpm actions still target Node 20. They are warnings only for now, but bumping to v5 or v6 of those actions is a cheap follow-up.

The skill is on main. Written for the Claude Code agent working in this repo.

**What I built:** `.claude/skills/production-readiness/`, registered in CLAUDE.md's skill list.

- **SKILL.md** (231 lines) holds the scale contract, three tiers, and a triage table of all 94 concepts. Each row gives a tier and a one-line decision for this stack. It also has checklists by kind of change: Server Action or Route Handler, migration, anything calling the Claude API, cron, money writes, deploy, and new dependency. It ends with escalation triggers and an anti-pattern list for pushing back on Redis, queues, Kubernetes and friends.
- **references/** has one file per category with the how-to for every concept: what it is, how it maps to Next.js 16 plus Supabase on a serverless host, and the concrete move. The agent reads a reference only when a concept is in play, so the always-loaded part stays small.

**The tiers.** Do means apply in the same PR. Cheap version means a Postgres table or platform toggle gives most of the value. Know means design so it can be added later and write one sentence in the PR instead of code. I verified both the table and the references cover exactly 94 entries.

**Examples of the calibration.** Rate limiting is a Postgres counter, not Redis. Queues and dead-letter queues are a jobs table with a failed status. Sagas and distributed transactions are replaced by one Postgres function per user action. Idempotency, backups, timeouts, CSRF checks on cookie-authenticated routes and region co-location are all Do, because skipping them costs real data or real money even at 60 users.

**Two things you should know.**

- **The repo is public.** The committed session transcript from Pratyush's session, and every future log the hook writes, is world-readable. You accepted logging for Jarso sessions; I want to be sure you accepted it as public. The hook also wrote an empty log for my session, with zero exchanges captured, so I left it uncommitted.
- **Main moved while I worked.** Pratyush pushed a three-line gitignore change. I read it, rebased my commit on top and pushed.

The skill-creator workflow can also run test prompts against the skill and tune its trigger description. That is optional and costs tokens, so I skipped it unless you want it.

All of it is on main and CI is green on the final commit, including the new gitleaks job.

**Five layers now stand between a credential and the public repo or its logs.**

1. **The agent cannot see secrets.** The project settings deny reading any `.env` file, dumping Supabase status or secrets, pulling host env vars, and pushing schema to the hosted database. A transcript cannot leak what the agent never read.
2. **The log hook redacts before writing.** Supabase, Anthropic, OpenAI, GitHub, AWS, Google and Slack key formats, JWTs, connection-string passwords, bearer tokens, PEM blocks, `KEY=value` lines and `"password": "…"` literals are masked, and non-GitHub email addresses are removed. I tested 19 fabricated secret shapes and 13 placeholder cases. Both existing logs pass through unchanged.
3. **Pre-commit refuses secrets.** A shell scan runs first in the Husky hook and blocks staged `.env` files and credential-shaped strings in added lines.
4. **CI runs gitleaks** over the full history on every push and PR, with Supabase and Anthropic rules added.
5. **GitHub itself** now has secret scanning, push protection, Dependabot alerts and private vulnerability reporting enabled on the repo. Dependabot also watches npm and Actions versions weekly.

**Application hardening.** The API wrapper now rejects cross-origin mutations with a 403, with a unit test covering same-origin, preview hosts, missing Origin and a forged cross-site request.

**Rules and docs.** CLAUDE.md gained rule 17 on public-repo conduct. A new `public-repo-security` skill tells the agent how to behave when every transcript is published, including what to do the moment a key appears. `docs/security.md` holds the policy, the table of where each secret lives, and a rotation runbook. A root `SECURITY.md` gives judges and researchers a reporting path. The security-auditor agent now checks the public surface too.

**Two honest notes.**

- **Commit history is messier than intended.** The pre-commit scan correctly stopped my first commit chain on a false positive in my own doc text, but that left everything staged, so the whole change landed as one commit under the redaction message, followed by two small fixes. I did not force-push to repair it, since main is shared.
- **Branch protection is not on.** You and Pratyush both push to main directly, so requiring PRs would change your workflow. It is the one remaining GitHub-side control worth turning on before judging, and I can do it if you want it.

The Stop hook will refresh this session's log when I finish, so expect a modified file under `.claude-logs/` to commit with the next change.

Everything is in hand. The scan found nothing: no keys, no non-GitHub emails, no local paths, and the session log the PR adds was produced by the redacting hook. The pr-review skill is committed and pushed to main. Here is the review, written the way the skill prescribes.

**Verdict: merge after two consistency fixes.** They are each a sentence or two, but a requirements document whose headline rule contradicts its own body will be implemented inconsistently, and this document is explicitly meant to be read before anyone touches ledger code.

**Safety scan:** clean. **CI:** all five checks passed, including gitleaks and the Semantic PR title check, which is now active because the workflow is on main. The branch includes current main. No force pushes.

**Blocking**

- **The headline rule contradicts gifts.** REQUIREMENTS.md line 9 and the "Core rule" in domain.md say a balance counts *only* parent-confirmed handovers. FR04 lets the child record a gift, says balances update accordingly, and the glossary lists gifts as recorded money. The "Money rules" section already states the rule correctly, scoped to allowance, withdrawal and emergency money. Reword the two headline sentences to match it, or decide the open gift question now.
- **The first milestone needs notifications FR16 does not give it.** Milestone step 6 and the acceptance criteria require a limit notice, and step 7 requires the parent to learn of a request and decide it. FR16 scopes only the due badge and handover reminder to the first milestone. Add limit notices and new-request notices to that sentence, in-app only, or drop them from the milestone.

**Should fix**

- **Spend to Save transfers have no state model.** FR03 says the transfer posts when the parent confirms receiving the cash, which means a pending state exists, but nothing defines what the child sees meanwhile, where it lives, or who can cancel it. Withdrawals got a full pending, approved, handed-over, rejected model. Either give transfers the same treatment under FR14 and the `requests` module, or record them immediately. The session log shows the agent inferred this rule and asked for it to be checked. It was not checked.
- **FR08 is missing from "Rest of the pilot".** The later-versions bullet says topic material stays in the pilot, but the pilot list omits FR08.
- **Role must not be self-assignable.** domain.md tells implementers to add a role to the profile. The existing profile update schema and `PATCH /api/v1/me` let a user edit their own profile, so one line is needed: role and family links are set server-side and never appear in a user-writable schema. This is where a child becomes a parent otherwise.

**Nit**

- The `profiles ✅` row maps to FR13, but nothing about approval gating or roles exists yet. "Partial" is more honest.
- Quarterly and annual allowances mean the due-badge flow may never run for some families during a pilot. Worth noting, not changing.

**Questions for the author**

- The log shows three sources: an externally drafted document, Pratyush's five answers, then a pasted paragraph that reversed one answer, turning over-limit approval into a notice. The final text follows that paragraph. Was that a team decision, or a second draft from the same outside tool?
- What may a child enter before parent approval? Ages 11 to 14 include under-13s, and today's sign-up collects a name and email from whoever signs up. The open-decisions list covers verification but not pre-approval data collection.
- Do pending or approved withdrawals reserve Save balance for goal progress, or only at handover?

**What is good.** The handover model is coherent, the idempotency and exactly-once language maps directly onto the append-only ledger design, FR numbers are preserved so nothing else in the repo breaks, and the open decisions are stated instead of hidden.

Nothing is posted on GitHub. Say the word and I will post this as a review requesting the two changes.

Deployed and verified. `https://jarso.vercel.app` is live on the scaffold.

**What is running**

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Home, login, sign-up | 200 |
| `/dashboard` signed out | 307 to login with return path, via the proxy against the hosted Supabase |
| `/api/health` | 200 with the expected body |
| `/api/v1/me` signed out | 401 |
| Unknown route | 404 |
| Security headers | HSTS, nosniff, frame deny, referrer and permissions policies all present |
| CSRF guard | PATCH from a foreign origin gets 403 |
| Function region | every function in `hnd1`, Tokyo, same region as the Supabase project |

Env vars for production and preview are set in Vercel: app URL, Supabase URL and publishable key. The secret key is deliberately not set, since nothing uses it yet. A `docs/deploy.md` with the full setup, env table, verification commands and rollback steps is on main, linked from the README.

**Two steps only you can do**

1. **Supabase auth URLs.** In Authentication, URL Configuration, set the Site URL to `https://jarso.vercel.app` and add `https://jarso.vercel.app/auth/callback` to the redirect URLs. Until then, sign-up confirmation emails from production will not land back in the app. I could not do this by API because the keychain blocked the Supabase token behind a GUI prompt.
2. **Connect Git in Vercel.** Project Settings, Git, connect `harisshakeel/Jarso`. The permission classifier blocked me from doing that as an account change. Once connected, pushes to main deploy automatically and PRs get preview URLs. Until then deploys are manual with `vercel deploy --prod`.

No, it is not connected to GitHub. Here is exactly what happened.

**How the deployment was created.** I ran `vercel deploy --prod` from your local checkout. The Vercel CLI uploaded the working tree from this Mac directly to Vercel, which built it in the cloud and promoted it to production. GitHub was not involved at any point. That is why the production deployment shows `harisshakeel` as the username rather than a commit from the repo.

**What that means today**

- Vercel does not know about `harisshakeel/Jarso`. A push to main does nothing on Vercel, and PRs get no preview URLs.
- The deployed code is whatever was in my working tree at that moment. It matches main as of the `vercel.json` commit, but there is no commit SHA attached to the deployment and no guarantee of that link going forward.
- Every future deploy would be a manual `vercel deploy --prod` from someone's machine until Git is connected.

**Why I did not connect it.** I tried `vercel git connect` right after the deploy. The permission classifier in this session blocked it as an account-level change, so I stopped and flagged it rather than finding a way around. I should have made that distinction clearer in the summary.

**How to connect it.** Two options:

1. **In the dashboard, about a minute.** Vercel, project `jarso`, Settings, Git, Connect Git Repository, pick `harisshakeel/Jarso`, production branch `main`. If the Vercel GitHub app is not yet installed on your account, Vercel will prompt to install it with access to that one repo.
2. **I run it with your approval.** Say so and I will re-run `vercel git connect` here. It needs the same GitHub app installation, so you may still get a browser prompt.

Once connected, the next push to main produces a Git-linked production deployment that supersedes the manual one, and `docs/deploy.md` already describes that as the normal path.

Written for: whoever builds Jarso's UI next, you, Pratyush or the agent.

Done and on main. Vercel will have rebuilt production from the push, though nothing visible changes yet since the tokens are documented, not applied.

**Logos** are in `public/brand/`, the folder Next.js serves static files from: the jar mark as `jarso-mark.png` and the wordmark as `jarso-wordmark.png`. One caveat: the wordmark is not transparent. It has a near-white baked-in background, so it only works on white surfaces until the designer exports an SVG. The two files also differ slightly in colour. I based the palette on the mark, since it becomes the app icon a child sees every day, and noted both points as open items for the designer.

**The design system** is at `docs/design.md`, linked from the docs index, CLAUDE.md's reading list, and the UI skill, so any agent touching UI reads it first. It encodes all twelve of your answers. The highlights:

- **Colour.** Brand teal, gold and cyan measured from the pixels, expanded into 50 to 950 tonal scales. Primary is `teal-700` rather than the raw brand teal, because the brand value is only 4.95:1 on white while `teal-700` is 6.9:1 both as text and under white button text. Gold is a fill colour with ink text, never text on white, with `gold-600` as its text form. Dark mode flips primary to `teal-400` and lets brand gold become a text colour at 10.5:1. Every ratio in the document is measured, not guessed.
- **Money and status.** Teal for Spend, gold for Save and goals, cyan for "due", dashed gold for "reserved", neutral for pending and rejected. Red never touches an amount, so a child is never shamed by colour.
- **Type.** Nunito throughout, with a money scale in tabular numerals so amounts line up. A one-time check that Nunito's tabular figures align is called out.
- **Shape and layout.** 14 px control radius giving roughly 20 px cards, 4 px spacing grid, 360 px first, bottom tab bar with child and parent tab sets, 44 px targets.
- **Components, motion, voice, role emphasis, accessibility** each have a section, and section 13 is a ready-to-paste `globals.css` token block for light and dark.

**Next step, when you say go:** apply it. Section 14 lists the order: Nunito in the root layout, the token block into `globals.css`, app icons exported from the mark, the missing shadcn primitives, then the brand components `JarsoMark`, `JarIllustration`, `Money` and `StatusChip`, and finally the tab-bar shell. That is the "create a style for the UI" step and needs one consent to run `pnpm install` locally so Biome and typecheck can run before pushing, or I push and let CI verify as before.

Written for: whoever builds Jarso's UI next, you, Pratyush or the agent.

The design document is updated on main. No video was needed: the sites' HTML and CSS gave exact fonts, colour tokens, radii, shadow recipes and keyframes, and their Open Graph images showed the look.

**What the four references have in common, and what changed because of it**

- **Warm paper canvas replaces the cool teal-tinted background.** 8x, Karma and AFK all sit on an off-white like `#F6F5F2` with near-black ink and one accent. Jarso now uses paper `#F7F5F0`, ink `#141C1F`, white cards, and lets teal and gold be the only colour on the page. Dark mode is unchanged.
- **Hairline cards and soft deep shadows.** Karma's recipe, a one-pixel ink-alpha ring plus a long low-opacity drop shadow, becomes our resting and raised elevation tokens. They work on paper and on white without separate border colours.
- **Pill action buttons, 14 px inputs.** All three products use pills for standalone actions next to 20 to 24 px cards. Adopted, with the 14 px radius kept for form controls.
- **Keycap button from AFK.** A three-pixel ledge under the child's single primary action per screen, dropping on press. Gold keycap only for money moments like confirming a handover.
- **Motion vocabulary.** Rise-in entrances staggered by 40 ms, a shimmer skeleton, AFK's coin flip for the confirmation toast, blurred sticky bars. Smooth-scroll libraries and parallax explicitly left to marketing.
- **Type and voice.** Tight large headlines, a sentence-case eyebrow style for steps and sections, and the two-beat headline cadence: "Due today. Hand it over."
- **Section 15** records each site, its palette and type, what we take and what we leave. Your portfolio contributed typographic confidence; its dark palette stays out of the app.

**Contrast was re-measured on the new canvas,** and the gate caught two misses. Cyan-600 as text drops to 4.4:1 on paper, so light-mode cyan text is now cyan-700 at 6.2:1. Muted text on chips measured 4.49:1, so it darkened to 5.2:1. Every pairing in the document is a measured value, and the token block in section 13 reflects all of it.

Since 8x and Karma look like the host's own products, Jarso will sit comfortably beside them in judging while the jar, teal and gold keep it distinct. Next step when you say go is applying section 14: fonts, tokens, icons, primitives, brand components, then the tab-bar shell.

| Step | Scope | Size |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0a | Fix add-child on hosted. Pratyush, in progress. | hours |

The research changed the diagnosis. Reading the actual GoTrue source: the Admin API's `createUser` only checks email syntax and never runs the domain validator, so the `.invalid` address is **not** the cause, and neither is the `email_address_invalid` code Pratyush expected. The domain validator only runs when an email is actually sent, and child accounts never send one. That leaves two realistic suspects: the hosted password policy rejecting the hex password, or one of our own database triggers on `auth.users` failing on hosted, which GoTrue reports as a generic "Database error creating new user". The code already logs the exact code and status, so one real attempt settles it.

**Your part for 0a, about a minute:** on http://localhost:3000, sign in with your parent account, open Add child, submit a nickname and PIN. I will read the error from the dev server log; nothing in it is printed to this chat except the code and status.

**Your part for 0b, in the Supabase dashboard,** from the checklist in `docs/auth.md`:

1. Authentication, URL Configuration: Site URL `https://jarso.vercel.app`; redirect URLs `https://jarso.vercel.app/auth/callback**` and `http://localhost:3000/auth/callback**`.
2. Authentication, Email Templates, Confirm signup: subject `Confirm your Jarso account`, body copied from `supabase/templates/confirmation.html`.
3. Authentication, Providers, Email: Confirm email on, Secure email change on, Secure password change on. Leave password strength at the defaults.

Leftover test users: the classifier blocked me from even listing hosted users, so deletion is yours too. In Authentication, Users, remove the `smoke…@gmail.com` accounts and any `child.jarso` entries from Pratyush's tests.

The override block now lives in `supabase/config.toml` under `[remotes.production]`, so future pushes keep hosted values pinned and can never downgrade production to the local settings.

**Two free-tier limits you need to know about,** both from Supabase's own docs and the push error:

- Custom email templates are refused on the free tier with the default email provider. Pratyush's `/auth/confirm` any-browser link cannot be used until custom SMTP exists; the default template only works in the browser that started the sign-up.
- The default provider only delivers to addresses that belong to the project's team, and caps at two emails an hour. No parent outside the team can confirm a sign-up until custom SMTP is configured. Resend offers SMTP once a sending domain is verified. This is the first real blocker for the pilot and belongs on the plan ahead of slice 2.

**What the log shows for 0a.** You signed up on localhost and the confirmation link came back `otp_expired` twice. That usually means a mail scanner opened the link first and consumed the token, which also means the account is likely already confirmed. Try signing in at `localhost:3000/login`. If it says the email is not confirmed, confirm the user by hand in Authentication, Users. Then add a child and tell me; the error code lands in the dev log and I will read it.

Root cause found and fixed on the Vercel side. The last step needs a new deployment, which is yours or the next merge's.

**What the logs showed.** Production's `POST /start` returned a handled error with nothing in the function logs, while the same `hit_rate_limit` call had succeeded from localhost during the child sign-in. So the hosted database was fine and the difference was production's environment.

**Root cause.** The Supabase CLI masks secret keys in its output unless `--reveal` is passed. I had not passed it, so the value I piped into Vercel this morning was a 41-character placeholder with an invalid character set, not the key. Every admin call on production failed with a generic message, and "Failed to check rate limit" was simply the first admin call the child-start form makes. Localhost works because you pasted the real key from the dashboard.

**Fix applied.** The variable was removed and re-created for production and preview from the revealed CLI output, piped without ever being printed, and verified by shape before the write. The listing now shows both created seconds ago. No value appeared in this chat or in any log.

**What is still needed.** Vercel applies env changes to new deployments only, and redeploying production is blocked for me. Either click Redeploy on the latest production deployment in Vercel, or approve the small PR below, whose merge produces the deployment. Then try "Ask my parent" on `jarso.vercel.app/start` again; it should go through.

**Pending your approval, one PR:** `toAppError` now logs the Postgres or PostgREST code and message for unexpected database failures, which would have made this a one-minute diagnosis instead of a hunt, and `docs/deploy.md` records the `--reveal` requirement. PR #16 is green on every check: lint, typecheck, gitleaks, pgTAP with the 31 new assertions, and the e2e suite including the three settings and removal flows. https://github.com/harisshakeel/Jarso/pull/16

**What is in it,** five commits, no co-author lines: the feature with migration, types, primitive, components, pages and tests; the database-error logging and deploy note, with the docs folded in by the commitlint hiccup; the session log; and the CI fix that loosened the nickname constraint so Pratyush's existing test stays valid.

**Two things before the merge**

1. **The migration deploy you asked for in CI.** The classifier refused to let me create a production-deploy workflow, so here it is for you to add as `.github/workflows/deploy-db.yml`. It runs on every merge to main that touches migrations, and warns instead of failing until the two secrets exist. Add `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` (supabase.com, Account, Access Tokens) and `SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD` (Project Settings, Database) under the repo's Actions secrets. If the workflow is on main before #16 merges, the merge applies the migration automatically; otherwise `pnpm db:push` by hand once.

```yaml
name: Deploy database
on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths: ["supabase/migrations/**", ".github/workflows/deploy-db.yml"]
concurrency:
  group: deploy-db
  cancel-in-progress: false
permissions:
  contents: read
env:
  SUPABASE_PROJECT_REF: sbnakvwvaymzypwmpqzv
jobs:
  push:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 15
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
      - id: secrets
        env:
          TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN }}
          PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD }}
        run: |
          if [ -z "$TOKEN" ] || [ -z "$PASSWORD" ]; then
            echo "::warning::SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN or SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD not set; migrations NOT applied."
            echo "ready=false" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          else
            echo "ready=true" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          fi
      - uses: supabase/setup-cli@v1
        if: steps.secrets.outputs.ready == 'true'
        with:
          version: 2.122.0
      - if: steps.secrets.outputs.ready == 'true'
        env:
          SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN }}
          SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD }}
        run: |
          supabase link --project-ref "$SUPABASE_PROJECT_REF"
          supabase db push --linked --yes
          supabase migration list --linked
```

2. **Questions from the review you may want to weigh in on,** none blocking: the parent home still shows only Reset PIN, with rename and remove living in settings, which keeps the home calm but means a parent has to find the gear once; and settings mutations have no rate limit, which I left out deliberately at pilot scale.

Say the word and I squash-merge #16 the same way as before. The plan for feature 2, parent profile completion, is already written at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-29-parent-profile-completion.md` and starts on a fresh branch right after.

Claude Code's own permission system. This session runs in auto mode, where a built-in safety classifier screens each shell command before it executes and can deny it with a reason. It is not something in your repo or on Supabase or Vercel.

Across this session it has refused a handful of actions, each with a stated category:

- **Production Deploy:** redeploying production on Vercel, and today, writing the `deploy-db.yml` workflow, since the file's purpose is to push schema changes to the live database.
- **Credential Materialization:** writing the Supabase keys into your `.env.local`.
- **Production Reads / Modify Shared Resources:** listing or creating users on the hosted Supabase project from a script.
- **Code from External:** running the shadcn registry fetch, and once a chained `pnpm` command.
- **Unauthorized Persistence:** activating the Husky git hooks.
- **Account & Standing-Rule Changes:** the first attempt at `vercel git connect`.

In each case I stopped, did the safer part of the task, and handed the rest to you, which is what the denial asks for and what your standing rules want. The classifier is conservative by design and sometimes over-triggers; writing a YAML file is harmless on its own, but it judges the intent.

If you want these to go through in future, you can grant them explicitly. The `update-config` skill can add permission rules to the project's `.claude/settings.json`, for example allowing `vercel redeploy` or writes under `.github/workflows/`. I will not change those settings on my own, and I would still ask before any production push, since the repo's own deny list and your commit-approval rule stay in force either way.

The access token is needed; the database password, it turns out, is not. I had that wrong.

**Why the workflow needs credentials at all.** A GitHub Actions run starts on an empty machine. To apply a migration it has to do two things on your behalf:

- **Find and talk to the project.** The Supabase CLI uses the Management API for that, and the API only answers to an access token. On your Mac the CLI already has one in the keychain from `supabase login`, which is why `pnpm db:push` works there without asking.
- **Run the SQL.** Migrations execute inside Postgres, which needs a database login.

**Why the password is optional.** I checked the pinned CLI. When no database password is given, it uses the access token to create a short-lived login role and connects with that. It is what printed "Initialising login role" every time a migration command ran from your terminal. So the token alone covers both steps.

**What I'd change, pending your OK:** make the workflow need only `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, pass the password only if someone sets it, and drop the password from the docs and PR notes. That leaves you one secret to add, generated at supabase.com → Account → Access Tokens. One small commit on PR #16.

**If you'd rather not store any Supabase credential in GitHub:** skip the workflow and keep running `pnpm db:push` yourself after each merge that adds a migration. That works fine at this scale. The cost is that someone has to remember, and production breaks when they forget.

Here is the flow as it stands today, from a change to production. Correct any step that doesn't match what you want.

**For a change without a database migration** (UI, logic, docs):

1. Make the change on a branch.
2. Open a PR. CI runs lint, typecheck, unit tests, build, the database tests and end-to-end tests. Vercel builds a preview.
3. You review, then squash-merge.
4. Vercel deploys `main` to production automatically.

Nothing else. This is what PRs #14 and #15 went through.

**For a change with a migration** (like PR #16, which adds the rename and remove functions):

1. to 3. are the same. CI also proves the migration works on a fresh test database.
4. Vercel deploys the new app code to production.
5. **The hosted database still has the old schema.** Someone has to apply the migration, or the new features fail on production.

Step 5 is the only open question, and there are two ways to do it:

- **Manual:** after merging, run `pnpm db:push` from your machine. No setup. You or Pratyush must remember it every time, and Pratyush's earlier PRs said "run `pnpm db:push`" in their deploy notes for exactly this reason.
- **Automatic:** the workflow I added runs `db push` itself when the merge lands. It needs one Supabase access token stored as a GitHub secret, added once.

So the whole question is: who applies migrations to production, a person after merging, or GitHub automatically?

**Automatic, one time setup:**

1. Go to supabase.com → your avatar → Account → Access Tokens, and create a token named `jarso-github-ci`. Copy it; Supabase shows it only once.
2. On GitHub, open the Jarso repo → Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → New repository secret. Name it `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, paste the token, save.
3. Done. From then on, every merge to `main` that includes a migration applies it to the hosted database by itself. You can watch it under the repo's Actions tab, in the run named Deploy database.

One catch: the workflow currently waits for both the token and the database password, so with only the token it would skip. It needs a two-line change on PR #16 to run on the token alone. Say yes and I'll commit it to the PR.

**This one time, manually:**

1. Squash-merge PR #16.
2. In your terminal on the Jarso folder:

```bash
git checkout main && git pull
pnpm db:push
```

It lists the pending migration (`family_settings`) and asks you to confirm. Answer yes.

Order doesn't matter much. If the token is already set when you merge, the workflow applies it first and your `pnpm db:push` just reports nothing to do. Running both is harmless.

Yes, I'd switch, with one check first and one thing to fix.

**Why it's better for us.** Supabase's GitHub integration applies new migrations itself when you merge to `main`. No Supabase token stored in GitHub, no workflow file to maintain, and Supabase reports each deploy's status and logs in its dashboard. It replaces exactly what my workflow does.

**The check first: whether it works on our Free plan.** The Supabase docs say deploying from GitHub works "on any plan". Their pricing page says Branching is not included on Free. Paid Branching is the per-PR preview databases, a separate feature. The pages don't settle whether "Deploy to production" is available on Free, so look in the dashboard:

1. Supabase → Project Settings → Integrations → GitHub Integration → Authorize GitHub.
2. Choose `harisshakeel/Jarso`, working directory `.`, production branch `main`.
3. If a **Deploy to production** switch is there, turn it on. Leave automatic preview branches off.

**The thing to fix: our config file block.** On each merge the integration also syncs auth settings from `supabase/config.toml` whenever a `[remotes]` block names the project, and ours does. That's mostly good, since auth settings become code. But it would also try to push the confirmation email template, which the free tier rejects, and I can't tell from the docs whether that fails the whole deploy. The first deploy shows it; if it does fail, I move the template out of the synced settings.

**If the switch is there:**

- I remove `deploy-db.yml` from PR #16 and point the docs at the integration, in one commit.
- Merging #16 then becomes the integration's first run and applies the migration.
- I confirm the result with `supabase migration list --linked`.

**If it isn't there:** we keep the workflow, which needs only the one access token.

Tell me what you see on the Integrations page.

The security review is handled: no critical or high findings, and the fixes for what it raised are in place, still uncommitted. The architecture review is still running; I'll fold in its findings when it reports.

**What security found and what I did:**

- **Medium, deploy order.** If the code reaches production before the migration, every parent page fails. No data is exposed, but it is an outage. This is the `pnpm db:push` before merge I flagged. Since Vercel previews use the production database, a preview of this branch will also error for parents until the migration is applied.
- **Low, invitation links.** A parent with an incomplete profile opening a child's invitation got the error page. Fixed: the invite page now says "Finish your profile first" and the dialog takes over.
- **Low, the docs overclaimed.** They said the block "holds even if the dialog is bypassed". The app server enforces it, but the database doesn't yet: a parent calling existing family functions directly through the Supabase API could skip it, and they're already authorised for those actions anyway. Docs corrected. The database check comes with the money functions, and I noted it in the ADR so the ledger work picks it up.
- **Low, unlisted exceptions.** The docs now name the deliberate exceptions: completing the profile, signing out, and editing your own display name.

**A product point for you, not a bug.** The app stores the full date of birth with no deletion date, although only "18 or over" is actually used. Gender is collected only for pilot analytics. Two options:

- **Store less:** keep only the birth year once the age check passes.
- **Tell parents:** keep full dates and make sure a privacy notice explains it.

Lint and typecheck pass after these changes.

Yes. That's the real fix, and the repo is already built for it. Each developer should run their own Supabase locally in Docker, and production should only change through merged PRs. Today everyone points at the one hosted project, so a `db:push` meant for testing lands on production, which is exactly what happened.

**How it would work:**

- `pnpm db:start` runs a full Supabase on your own machine: database, auth and Studio. `pnpm db:reset` rebuilds it from the migrations in the repo whenever you like. `docs/database.md` already describes this, and CI uses the same setup for every PR.
- `.env.local` points at the local stack instead of production. Pratyush's own lessons file says the app must never be run against the hosted keys for development.
- Each developer gets a private database and can try unmerged migrations freely. Nobody else is affected.
- Production gets migrations only after a PR merges, through the Supabase GitHub integration once it's connected, or one deliberate `pnpm db:push` from `main`. Never from a feature branch.

**What it needs:**

- **Docker on your Mac**, which isn't installed. Docker Desktop is the standard; OrbStack is a lighter alternative. Either is an installer you'd run yourself, given your rules about installers.
- **A team rule plus a guard:** I'd rename the `db:push` script to `db:push:prod`, make it refuse to run off `main`, and write the rule into `CLAUDE.md` and the docs. That's a small PR.

But every parent action now fails with "Failed to load your details": creating a family, adding a child, resetting a PIN. Production logs show no such errors in the last two hours, so nobody has tried one yet.

**Fastest fix: Pratyush runs one command.** Only his machine has those five migration files. From a checkout with his branch and the latest `main` together, he runs `pnpm db:push`. The CLI sees his five as already applied and applies only `parent_details`. That fixes production within a minute.

**Then the permanent fix:** he puts the five migration files in a PR exactly as they ran, and it merges, so `main` matches production. Until then, every merge that adds a migration will fail the same way.

I'm not applying the migration myself another way, for example by running its SQL directly. That would get around the repo's rule against the agent changing production, and it wouldn't record the migration properly, so the next deploy would try to apply it again and fail.

Build **purchases and limits** next. It's slice 3 of the milestone plan. Right now the child side is read-only: a child can see their jars and history but can't do anything. Recording a purchase closes the loop the landing page promises: the parent gives an allowance, the child spends some and saves some, and the parent sees it.

**The demo story it gives you:**
1. The parent sets an allowance starting today. It's due straight away, with no wait for the hourly job.
2. The parent hands it over and splits it between Spend and Save.
3. The child records a purchase. Spend drops, and it shows in both histories.
4. The child tries to spend more than they have and gets a friendly "not enough in Spend" message.
5. The parent sets a weekly limit, the child goes over it, and the purchase appears in the parent's "Needs you" list.

**Scope for one day:**
- **Must:** the child records a purchase with an amount and label. Overspending is blocked unless the parent allows it. Purchases show in history.
- **Should:** one spending limit per child, with over-limit purchases flagged on the parent's home. A simple notice there stands in for the full inbox.
- **Cheap extra:** money the child receives, like a gift from grandma, counting immediately. It reuses the same entry logic.

The work splits cleanly between two people. One builds the database function, limits table and tests. The other builds the purchase and limits screens.

**Leave for after the demo:**
- **Deposits and withdrawals.** They need request, approval and reservation logic, the riskiest money code in the milestone. That's too much for one day.
- **The notification inbox.** Due badges and "Needs you" already carry the demo.
- **Savings goal.** It would look good on the child screen, but it isn't in the first milestone. Add it only if purchases land early.

**Demo prep, alongside the build:**
- **Demo family:** set it up on production tonight through the app, with a couple of handovers and purchases, so the screens aren't empty. There's no demo clock yet, so any history has to exist before the demo.
- **Rehearsal:** run the whole flow in two browsers, one parent and one child, ideally on a phone.
- **Code freeze:** freeze `main` a few hours before the demo. Anything not merged by then waits.

My advice: don't switch before the demo. A Mastra switch is possible without much disruption, but it gives us almost nothing the guide uses, and it would mean running the safety evals again from scratch.

## What we use today

The guide is not an agent. Each turn makes three single calls to Claude:
1. A safety screen on the child's message, run on Haiku.
2. The answer, from Opus, at low effort.
3. A safety screen on the answer, also on Haiku.

All three return structured output. There are no tools, no agent loop and no streaming. We don't stream on purpose: the child sees nothing until the answer has passed the second screen. Chat history, retention and parent access live in our own Supabase tables, under RLS and our migrations.

## Trade-offs

| | Anthropic SDK (now) | Mastra |
|---|---|---|
| **Model choice** | Claude only | Any provider by config. This is Mastra's real advantage. |
| **What it adds** | Nothing beyond the API | Agent loop, tools, workflows, memory, tracing, a local Studio UI. The guide uses none of these. |
| **Guardrails** | Our own screens, written for our rules (no product picks, no comments on the family's money, no giving the answer away early, never claims to be human) | Generic built-ins for PII, prompt injection and moderation. Each costs an extra model call, and none covers our rules, so we'd still write the screens ourselves. |
| **Speed** | The first safety screen runs at the same time as the answer | Mastra's input checks run before the model call, so every reply gets slower unless we keep our own wiring |
| **Claude features we rely on** | Used directly | All reachable through the Vercel AI SDK underneath Mastra, but behind two layers: effort, prompt caching, native structured output, and a system message in the middle of the chat (needs `allowSystemInMessages: true`). I couldn't confirm Mastra passes that last one through. |
| **Telling refusals from outages** | Exact: a refusal shows the child fixed copy, a network or HTTP error trips the circuit breaker | A refusal comes back as `content-filter` and Mastra has its own error types. We'd have to rebuild that split and re-test it. That's the most delicate part for a children's product. |
| **Dependencies** | One package | `@mastra/core` plus the AI SDK: a large dependency tree that releases very often (1.80.0 came out on Oct 28). That's more supply-chain risk, which matters given your Mac reset, and its setup step is `npx mastra@latest init`, which your rules don't allow. |

## How much would change

**Minimal swap (about a day, including the eval run):** we already have a seam for this.
- Only [claude.ts](src/features/guide/server/claude.ts) (181 lines) and its test import the SDK. Everything else talks to the `GuideProvider` interface in [provider.ts](src/features/guide/server/provider.ts).
- So we'd rewrite those two files, change a few lines in `config.ts`, and add the packages.
- Nothing else changes: the pipeline, database, migrations, RLS, cron, UI, budget, circuit breaker and evals all stay as they are.
- We'd then have to re-run and pass the 158-case eval gate, because every safety guarantee was measured against the current calls.

**Using Mastra properly (much bigger):**
- Mastra's memory, its chat route and streaming would replace our tables and server actions.
- Its memory storage creates tables outside our migrations and RLS policies, which breaks repo rule 7.
- Streaming breaks the screen-before-the-child-sees-it rule.
- In practice that means rewriting the guide and reopening ADR 0010.

## Bottom line

Mastra makes sense if the guide becomes a real agent, with tools such as looking up the child's jars or multi-step workflows, or if you want to run other providers. If what Pratish wants is model choice, we can get it later by adding a second `GuideProvider` built on the AI SDK alone, without the rest of Mastra. It's worth asking him what problem he wants Mastra to solve. If it's one of those, we can plan it after the demo.

The eval run still needs the Anthropic key in the `guide-evals` environment. Once it's in, tell me and I'll start it. I can also draft a short reply to Pratish if you'd like.

Sources:
- [AI SDK Anthropic provider](https://ai-sdk.dev/providers/ai-sdk-providers/anthropic)
- [Mastra agents overview](https://mastra.ai/docs/agents/overview)
- [Mastra structured output](https://mastra.ai/docs/agents/structured-output)
- [Mastra processors](https://mastra.ai/docs/agents/processors)
- [Mastra in Next.js](https://mastra.ai/docs/frameworks/web-frameworks/next-js)
- [Mastra releases](https://github.com/mastra-ai/mastra/releases)
- [Mastra 1.0 overview (DEV)](https://dev.to/jangwook_kim_e31e7291ad98/mastra-ai-10-the-typescript-agent-framework-developers-are-actually-shipping-2lan)
- [Mastra framework page](https://mastra.ai/ai-agent-framework)

I recommend staying on the Anthropic SDK for now and reconsidering Mastra only if one of the triggers at the end shows up.

## The use case right now

- About 60 pilot families, with children aged 11–14.
- The guide answers short money questions inside lessons: "Why?" on a step, help on a topic, and questions from Home.
- Every answer is safety-checked before the child sees it. Parents can switch the guide off and read the chats.
- It's a single model provider (Claude). The demo is soon, and the 158-case safety evals were built for the current code.

## Trade-offs for this use case

| What matters now | Anthropic SDK | Mastra |
|---|---|---|
| **Child safety** | ✅ We control exactly what happens on a refusal (fixed copy) versus an outage (circuit breaker), and every reply is checked before the child sees it. | ⚠️ Adds a layer in between. Its built-in guardrails are generic and don't cover our rules: no product picks, no comments on the family's money, no giving answers away. We'd still write our own screens and re-prove them. |
| **Reply speed** | ✅ The safety check runs at the same time as the answer. | ⚠️ Its input checks run before the model call, so each reply is slower. |
| **Cost per reply** | ✅ Three calls per turn, within the daily budget cap. | ⚠️ Same if we keep our screens; more if we add its guardrails, which each make an extra model call. |
| **Demo risk** | ✅ Built, merged, deployed. It only needs the key. | ❌ About a day of rewriting, plus a fresh eval run, the day before the demo. |
| **Children's data** | ✅ Chats sit in our tables under RLS, deleted after 30 days, readable by the parent. | ⚠️ Its memory feature keeps chats in its own tables, outside our migrations and policies, so we'd have to leave it off. |
| **Security surface** | ✅ One package. | ⚠️ A large dependency tree that releases every few days, and its setup step is a remote `npx` script. |
| **Switching models later** | ⚠️ Claude only. We already have a plug-in point for adding another provider in one file. | ✅ Any provider by config. |
| **Future agent features** | ⚠️ We'd build tool calls and multi-step flows ourselves. | ✅ Tools, workflows, tracing and a local playground come ready-made. |

Mastra wins only the last two rows, and the current use case needs neither. Everything that matters this week favours the SDK.

## When to reconsider Mastra

- The guide needs to **act**, not just answer. For example, it reads the child's jars or helps set up a savings goal through tool calls.
- We need **another model provider**. Even then, the AI SDK alone, added as a second provider in one file, is enough without the rest of Mastra.
- We need **tracing and dashboards** beyond our logs once usage grows past the pilot.

If one of those comes up after the demo, I'd plan the switch then and run it through the same eval gate.

**Where they sit in Demo 2:**

It explains what Jarso is and the family loop (allowance, jars, goal, learning), shows how children stay safe with a link to `/safety`, covers the money guide, and keeps one way in for kids and one for parents. We'd design it in Figma from the design doc first. It depends on nothing, so it can start now alongside the money work.
- **Gamified child dashboard (large):**
  - Child Home becomes the child's hub: the next step as today's mission, Spend and Save as jars that fill, the goal as a journey with milestones, skill badges (Started, Practised, Got it), and learning days this week.
  - It also takes FR12's recognition, which I moved into Demo 2 from the final demo.
  - It comes after the savings goal, because the goal is its centrepiece. Parent Home stays calm and gains each child's progress at a glance.

**Gamification rules** for an app used by 11–14-year-olds:
- Rewards follow understanding and good decisions, never how much money a child has.
- No losing streaks or "you missed a day" messages.
- No random rewards or loot boxes.
- No leaderboards between children.
- Celebrations only on child screens, and they respect reduced motion.

These come from FR12 and the design doc, and they're listed on the page.

The top of the roadmap now has a **timeline** with three tracks (money, learning and design, platform and ops). Demo 2 has six slices: the four money slices, then the two design slices, with demo hardening last. In the final demo, the weekly review becomes a card on the dashboard.

Only a parent removing their own child triggers it, and the per-child limit of 30 messages a day keeps the amount to a few dollars. The Console limit can't be affected by it. The child's guide stays hidden until two switches are on. After that, it appears in four places.

**1. Turn the guide on for the app**
- **Production:** in Vercel, set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `FEATURE_GUIDE=true`, then redeploy.
- **Locally:** your key is already in `.env.local`. Add `FEATURE_GUIDE=true` there too, then restart `pnpm dev`.

If either is missing, the guide doesn't appear anywhere, and parents don't see its switch either.

**2. The parent turns it on for the child**

Sign in as the parent, go to Home, and open the child's page. There's a **Money guide** card with the switch "Money guide for {nickname}". It's off by default. The card also links to that child's chats.

**3. Where the child finds it**

| Where | Button | What it opens |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Child Home | **Ask the money guide** | A general money chat |
| A topic page (Learn → a topic) | **Ask about this** | A chat about that topic |
| Inside a mission or practice step | **Help me** before answering, **Why?** after | Hints that hold back the answer until the child has tried twice, then an explanation of the answer |

The first time the child opens it, a short intro card explains what the guide is. After that it goes straight to the chat.

**If the button doesn't show:** either the app-level setting from step 1 is missing or the parent's switch is off. Changing the env vars needs a redeploy (or a dev server restart) before they take effect.

The child's chats are limited to 5 messages a minute, 30 a day and 12 turns per chat. The whole guide also pauses with a "taking a break" message once the US$10 daily or US$100 monthly cap is reached.

Written for: you and Pratyush, who build the page. The appendix prompts are written for ChatGPT.

**I couldn't find the skill you made.** It isn't in the repo's `.claude/skills/` or in any skill list this session can see. So I designed from [docs/design.md](docs/design.md) and the five reference sites. Tell me where the skill is and I'll apply it before we build anything.

The landing page design is in [2026-10-30-landing-page-design.md](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-30-landing-page-design.md). Nothing is committed.

**How I studied the sites.** I drove headless Chrome myself rather than reading their code alone. For each site I captured the hero at five moments over 7 seconds and 10–19 scroll positions at desktop and phone width. I also recorded which animations were running and pulled frames from AFK's hero video.

**What the five sites do that makes them not feel like templates:**
- **AFK** is the closest to your plan. Its hero is a 12-second video, and scrolling plays it forward and back.
  - Before you scroll, the first second loops gently.
  - People who turn off animations get a plain player with a play button.
  - The video ends on its first frame, so it loops cleanly.
- **Karma** pins the screen while three steps take turns, shows a product console with floating cards, and has a sentence that lights up word by word as you scroll.
- **Definable**:
  - its headline words blur in;
  - connector lines draw themselves between small chips;
  - a live console's numbers keep ticking;
  - every heading has a second half in grey.
- **What they share:** the product itself is the illustration, motion explains a sequence instead of decorating, and each section says one thing.

**The design, in short**
- **Hero:** "Spend some. Save some." over your video. Scrolling plays the money loop: allowance arrives, the jar splits into Spend and Save, a purchase, the savings ring fills, and back to the start. Four short captions appear in the page's own text, not inside the video.
- **How it works:** four pinned steps (handover, purchase, goal, mission). Each one is a real Jarso component playing a short demo with sample data, not a screenshot.
- **Money guide:** a typed chat demo, and a link to the safety page.
- **Parent and child side by side:** two phones, with a coin travelling from the parent's "Hand over" to the child's Save jar.
- **Safety:** one sentence that lights up as you scroll.
- **Close:** "Your turn." with the two ways in.
- **Size:** 190 words in total (Karma and 8x have about 750). No modals, cookie banner, FAQ wall, logo wall, gradient blobs or new libraries.

**GPT prompts** are in Appendix A of the spec:
1. Attach `public/brand/jarso-mark.png` and `jarso-wordmark.png` to the chat.
2. Paste the brand brief (A.1). It covers the logo, the exact colours, the style and a list of things to avoid.
3. Generate the still frame (A.2) and approve it.
4. Animate it (A.3): a timed 12-second script, camera locked, no text, last frame matching the first.
5. Dark and phone-shaped variants are in A.4.

**Before I build:**
1. **Four open questions** (spec §7):
   - keep "Spend some. Save some."?
   - £ or $ in the demos?
   - step 3 needs the savings goal, which isn't built yet: wait for it, or ship without that step?
   - make a dark version of the video, or keep the hero light in both themes?
2. **ffmpeg isn't installed.** The video has to be re-encoded so it plays smoothly while scrolling. I can do that if you approve `brew install ffmpeg`, or you can run it yourself.
3. **Figma.** The roadmap said Figma first, but I'd go straight to code: the page is mostly motion, and the components it shows already exist in code.
4. **Building** means running `pnpm dev` and `pnpm check`, which needs your OK for this goal.

The roadmap docs PR from earlier is still waiting for your go-ahead. I can put this spec in the same PR.

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:

   **Earlier in the session (before this portion):**
   - Learning track (#28) and guide (#29) shipped.
   - Guide monthly cap of US$100 (#32).
   - Roadmap docs drafted.

   **This portion, in order:**
   - **Landing page design.** Study the reference sites (8xresearch.com, karma.business, harisshakeel.site, afk-ai-tau.vercel.app, dev.definable.ai), including their animations and components. Keep Jarso branding. No "AI slop", no modal-style landing pages, minimal copy. Originally a GPT-generated hero video with branding and animation prompts (later dropped).
   - **Build it.** Use `/` as the landing page, with login and signup in the navbar.
   - **SEO.** Full metadata, sitemap, robots, a GPT video prompt with exact colours, and merge so it's live (done in #34).
   - **Hero rework (iterative feedback):**
     - Save jar not gold (deep teal).
     - No GPT video: a coded flat kid in Jarso style.
     - Opening shows only the kid, with H1 "Got money, but not sure how to manage it?" and buttons; question marks float when idle.
     - Coins thrown up; the view follows the coins and the kid exits.
     - Pop-ups "Spend some." (left), "Save some." (right), "Learn as you go." (centre).
     - The jar draws itself, the coins drop in, then the savings animation.
     - No ring or star, no bar: coins pour into Save and pile height is the progress.
     - Full coins on the merge.
     - Hero content centred; question marks without circles.
   - **The thread.** The jar outline crawls (stroke-draw, not snake or morph), keeping the hero jar's thickness everywhere including the timeline. It's never continuous (one moving stretch). It becomes the How it works line, weaves right around the family section and back left, slows on horizontals, and forms the closing "Your turn" jar.
   - **Navbar.** Remove "How it works"; one "Get started" button (menu). Wider oval coin on the rail.
   - **Ship it.** "Then commit it to the main", then "squash merge and get everything on main" (done: #35, #36).
   - **LATEST.** "now, coming back to the agent Jarso as an agent feature, we have to make it stand out. For that, we will be giving some user context to our Jarso. For that, we should keep the memory and also give access to our Jarso agent. Regarding the database, make a proper skill so that it can query and answer information in the database regarding the specific user. For example, it should be able to answer questions such as, 'How many more do I need to get the bicycle?' and any question it can ask related to the database information. Come up with a plan for how we will be doing this agent orchestration. This will be our major feature, and this will be in a Claude prompt window at the top of the dashboard, in the main area, so that we can show that our app is purely AI-powered."

2. Key Technical Concepts:

   **Stack and conventions**
   - Next.js 16 App Router, Cache Components (static landing prerender), Tailwind v4 (arbitrary values, `motion-safe`/`motion-reduce`, `data-[thread=travelling]:`).
   - Base UI Menu (`Menu.LinkItem` with `render={<Link/>}`), next-themes.
   - Biome, Vitest, Playwright, husky hooks (check-secrets, biome staged, typecheck, commitlint header ≤ 72 chars).
   - Squash-merge PR workflow.

   **Landing motion and SVG**
   - Scroll-driven SVG scenes as pure functions of progress; `useScrollProgress` (pinned/crossing, easing).
   - `useTimeline` (reduced motion returns `duration`); `useInView`; `useThreadState` (MutationObserver on `data-thread`).
   - Fixed-overlay "thread": a single SVG path built per frame from DOM rects and `getScreenCTM()`. A dash window (`strokeDasharray`/`strokeDashoffset`) slides along it.
   - `--thread-width` CSS variable on `documentElement`, with CSS fallbacks.
   - Pieces get scroll budgets (`CROSSING_PACE`) with a squeeze factor so the jar completes by the page end.

   **SEO and metadata**
   - Metadata API: `metadataBase`, `openGraph` replacing whole per page (so the image is named in `sharedOpenGraph`), `twitter`, `viewport.themeColor`.
   - File conventions: sitemap.ts, robots.ts, opengraph-image.png and twitter-image.png with `.alt.txt`.
   - JSON-LD via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` with `<` escaped.

   **Tooling**
   - Headless Chrome over CDP (Python stdlib websocket) for visual verification.

   **Guide feature context (relevant to the new agent request)**
   - ADR 0010: Claude Opus 5.5 (`claude-opus-5-5`) with structured output and Haiku 5.5 (`claude-haiku-5-5`) input and output screens.
   - `GuideProvider` interface; claude.ts is the only SDK file.
   - Budgets daily 1000¢ and monthly 10000¢; circuit breaker; rate limits 5/min, 30/day, 12 turns per chat.
   - Tables guide_access, guide_conversations, guide_messages, usage_counters.
   - FR09 boundaries: never asks for personal details, never recommends products, never comments on how much money a child or family has; parents read every chat; flag off by default plus a per-child switch.
   - The learning and guide spec listed "The guide reading balances, jars or goals" as a non-goal until those features exist.
   - FR02 savings goal isn't built yet (Demo 2 slice 3).

3. Files and Code Sections:

   **On main via #34 (f7bf515), #35 (d88b149), #36 (831e6fb)**

   - **src/app/(marketing)/page.tsx**
     - Composes StructuredData, LandingHeader, main (Hero, HowItWorks, GuideSection, FamilyView, LitStatement, YourTurn), LandingFooter.
     - Metadata: `title.absolute` = landingCopy.meta.title, `alternates.canonical "/"`, `openGraph {...sharedOpenGraph, title, description, url "/"}`.

   - **src/features/landing/** (barrel index.ts exports FamilyView, GuideSection, Hero, HowItWorks, LandingFooter, LandingHeader, LitStatement, StructuredData, YourTurn, landingCopy).
     - `copy.ts`:
       - hero.title "Got money, but not sure how to manage it?"
       - taglines ["Spend some.","Save some.","Learn as you go."]
       - captions ["Split it. Spend and Save.","Save for something real.","Again next week. A bit wiser."]
       - nav {safety, start "Get started", parents, logIn, setUp "Set up your family", kids, kidSignIn "Sign in", kidStart "Start here"}
     - `motion.ts`: clamp01, segment, lerp, easeOut, easeInOut, easeOutBack, drop, typed.
     - `jar-outline.ts`: JAR_SEGMENTS (7 segments from (150,128) to (418,170)), JAR_OUTLINE_D, jarOutlinePoints(count), JAR_OUTLINE_LENGTH.
     - `components/jar-art.tsx`:

I haven't built or committed anything for this yet; this is the plan for you to approve or change.

**The idea:** Jarso looks things up in the signed-in user's own data, remembers what matters to them, and answers from real figures. Money still moves only through the app's own screens. The AI explains, the code counts.

## 1. Build on the guide we already have

[features/guide](src/features/guide) already has most of the hard parts:
- the screened pipeline: Haiku input screen, Opus 5.5 main call, Haiku output screen;
- the parent's per-child switch, which is their consent;
- chats that parents can read;
- rate limits, the monthly budget, the circuit breaker and the evals.

The agent extends that module rather than replacing it, because a second module would duplicate all the safety work. Four things change:
- the main call becomes a short tool loop;
- it reads the user's data through tools;
- it gains memory;
- it moves from a sheet to a prompt window at the top of both homes.

## 2. The database skill: typed read-only tools, not SQL

A "skill" here is a pack of read-only tools plus the instructions for using them.

| Skill | Tools | When |
|---|---|---|
| Money | `get_jars`, `get_allowance`, `list_money_history(from, to, kind)`, `summarise_money(period)`, `get_rules`, `list_requests` | Now (requests after Demo 2 slice 1) |
| Goals | `get_goal` (saved, remaining, plan, payments left), `what_if(save_each)` | After FR02 |
| Learning | `get_learning` (skill levels, next step, reviews due), `find_topic` | Now |
| Memory | `remember(kind, text)`, `forget(id)` | Phase C |
| Family (parent only) | All of the above for one of the parent's children, plus `needs_you` | Phase E |

Every tool follows the same rules:
- **Runs as the signed-in user.** It uses the user's own Supabase client, so RLS applies; never the admin client.
- **The model never chooses whose data.** The child id comes from the session. A parent tool only accepts a child from that parent's own list.
- **Uses the existing services** (`getJars`, `getHistory`, `getSchedule`, `getLearningOverview`), so the layering rule holds.
- **Fixed shapes.** Inputs are checked with Zod and strict schemas, and results are capped in size.
- **All arithmetic happens in code:** remaining, payments left, weekly totals. The model never adds things up.
- **What a child typed is data.** Purchase labels and goal names are scrubbed and marked as data, so a label can't act as an instruction.
- **No write tools.**
- **Planned gifts never reach the child.** RLS already hides `parent_gifts` from children, so they never appear in the child's tools.

I'd avoid letting the model write SQL, and I'd avoid an Anthropic Agent Skill with code execution. Both give the model a path around RLS on children's data. A typed tool can be tested, gated and audited one at a time.

I'll also add a project skill, `.claude/skills/agent-tool`, with the checklist for adding a tool when a feature ships (scope, Zod, RLS test, eval case, status copy). That way the toolset grows with the app.

## 3. Memory

Memory is a list of structured facts, not free-form files.
- **Table:** a new `guide_memories` table holds the facts. Each fact has a kind (interest, thinking about, finds tricky, prefers) and text of at most 140 characters, and expires after 90 days. A child has at most 20 facts; the oldest drops off.
- **Examples:** "Thinking about saving for a bike, no goal yet", "Likes football examples", "Finds splitting tricky".
- **How it's written:** only through the `remember` tool, during a normal money question. Each fact is scrubbed and checked for personal details first. Nothing is ever remembered from a safety, personal-details or rule-breaking message.
- **Visible:** the chat shows "Jarso will remember: you're thinking about a bike · Undo". Both the child and the parent get a "What Jarso remembers" list where they can delete any fact.
- **Never stale:** balances and goals are never remembered. Jarso always fetches them fresh.

I'd skip Anthropic's memory tool here. It lets the model write any files it likes, and for children we need facts that are typed, checked, visible and expiring.

## 4. One turn: "How many more do I need to get the bicycle?"

1. **Checks:** role, the switch and its consent version, rate limits, budget and breaker; then the scrub. This is all existing code.
2. **Screen and agent start together.** As today, the Haiku input screen runs alongside the main call. The Opus loop starts with a small snapshot: today's date in the family's time zone, the currency, Spend and Save, the goal headline, the next allowance, the next learning step, and the child's memories. The snapshot leaves out the child's name and nickname.
3. **Tool call.** Opus calls `get_goal`, and the child sees "Checking your bicycle goal…". The result: saved 6400, remaining 5600, plan 800 from each allowance, 7 payments left.
4. **Reply with placeholders.** Opus writes "You need {goal.remaining} more. Saving {goal.plan} from each allowance, that's about {goal.payments_left} more allowances." It also proposes a card and a link to a topic.
5. **The server fills the figures in** from the tool results, the same idea as the existing `{m:1250}` content format. A raw number in the reply that isn't in the tool results blocks the reply, so a wrong figure can't reach a child.
6. **Output screen.** The Haiku output screen checks the reply against the tool results, then the child sees: "You need £56 more. Saving £8 from each allowance, that's about 7 more allowances."
7. **Stored for the parent:** the message, which tools ran, and the token counts. The parent's view shows "Checked: bicycle goal". Tool results are not stored.

The loop is capped at 3 tool rounds and 6 tool calls. I'd write the loop by hand in [claude.ts](src/features/guide/server/claude.ts) rather than use the beta Tool Runner, for four reasons:
- it has to stop the moment the input screen sends the message elsewhere;
- it has to check the budget each round;
- it has to send a status line for each tool;
- it has to run the same code against the fake provider in e2e.

Anthropic's hosted Managed Agents doesn't fit either. The data sits behind Supabase RLS and no sandbox is needed.

## 5. Safety changes (ADR 0011, plus edits to FR07 and FR09)

- **Talking about the child's own money.** FR09 and the output screen currently block any comment on how much money a child has. The new rule: Jarso may state the child's own figures factually when asked. It still never calls an amount small or big, never compares to anyone, and never tells the child to buy anything.
- **Fresh consent.** Today the switch covers sending the child's messages. Now money data goes too: balances, purchases and their labels, the allowance and learning progress. `guide_access` gets a consent version, so parents who already switched the guide on confirm again. The window tells the child what Jarso can see.
- **Parent chats stay private.** They are stored separately and only that parent can read them. The child-chat RLS would otherwise let the child read them.
- **Kept from ADR 0010:** no retry on another model when Claude refuses, plain-text history, 30-day retention, and logs without message text.
- **Lifts a non-goal** in the learning spec: "the guide reading balances, jars or goals".

## 6. The prompt window

- **Child Home:** the window goes at the top of the main area, above the next step: "Ask Jarso about your money…".
  - Three suggestion chips come from the child's data, for example "How long until my bike?" when a goal exists.
  - Answers open inline under the box, with no modal. While Jarso works, a status line names each lookup. Then come the reply, figure cards (the goal as a filling jar), action buttons, the quick check and a rating.
  - A line under the box: "Jarso is an AI. Your parent can read these chats."
- **Parent Home:** the same window, "Ask about your family". Chips like "What needs me this week?" and "How is Sam's saving going?".
- **Action buttons propose, never do.** They open a topic or mission, "Make it a goal" (the goal form, filled in), or "Ask to move money" (the request sheet, filled in). The person finishes the existing form; the agent never submits anything.
- **Streaming status only.** A Route Handler streams the status lines and then sends the screened reply in one piece. Reply text is never streamed, so ADR 0010's rule that nothing unscreened reaches a child still holds.
- **What stays:** the "Why?" help on lesson steps keeps the same engine. The roadmap's rule that UI slices start in Figma applies.

## 7. Models, cost and speed

- **Models:** Opus 5.5 at low effort runs the loop and Haiku 5.5 runs the screens, as now.
- **Caching:** the system prompt and tool definitions are identical on every request, so they stay cached. The per-turn context goes after the child's message.
- **Cost:** these are estimates I'll measure once it runs:
  - about 2¢ a question when the snapshot is enough;
  - about 4–6¢ with two tool rounds;
  - about 1¢ for today's guide.
  - The US$100 monthly cap would cover roughly 2,000–5,000 questions.
- **Speed:**
  - one round takes about 3–5 s;
  - two rounds take about 8–10 s, which the status lines cover.

## 8. Evals

I'll extend `evals/guide` with a test family whose child has £64 in Save and a £120 bicycle goal, saving £8 from each weekly allowance. The cases:
- every figure matches the fixture;
- no access to a sibling's money, a planned gift or another family;
- an injected instruction inside a purchase label is ignored;
- no judgement on "am I poor?";
- "remember my school is…" is refused;
- common questions take one round at most.

The gate: every figure grounded, zero data leaks, zero FR09 breaks, and at least 95% of answers judged helpful. Each tool also gets RLS tests.

## 9. Build order

| PR | What | Size | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | ADR 0011, FR07/FR09 edits, design spec, Figma frames | S | Your decisions below |
| B | Child agent core: the loop, money and learning skills, placeholders and the grounding check, status stream, consent version, the Home window, evals | L | A |
| C | Memory: table and RLS, remember and forget, "What Jarso remembers", expiry job | M | B |
| D | Goals skill: `get_goal`, `what_if`, "Make it a goal". This is the bicycle question | S | B + FR02 |
| E | Parent agent: family skill, private storage, the parent Home window | M | B |
| F | Eval gate run, fixes, demo script | S | B–E |

The bicycle example depends on the savings goal (FR02), which the roadmap puts after deposits and withdrawals.

## Decisions I need from you

1. **Child first, then parent?** I'd recommend it: the child version is the bicycle story and does the safety work once.
2. **Build the savings goal (FR02) earlier**, counting reserved money as zero until deposits and withdrawals land? Yes, if you want the bicycle in Demo 2.
3. **Memory:** structured facts (recommended) or Anthropic's memory tool.
4. **Budget and model:** questions will cost about 2–6× more. Do we keep the US$100 cap and 30 questions a day per child, or raise them? Opus 5.5 for everything is the default; Sonnet 5.5 for the loop would roughly halve the main-call cost, if you'd rather.
5. **Name:** Jarso speaks as "Jarso, the app's AI". The prompt currently gives it no name. The rules that it's an AI and never a person or a friend stay.

Once you decide, I'll write PR A, and I won't commit anything without your approval.

One question from earlier is still open: should I remove the line about which GitHub account is used for [private] from the public session log?

- A child closing the page mid-answer can no longer count towards the outage limit that switches Jarso off for everyone.

- Jarso will be off for the demo child until you turn it on again from the child's page, because the consent now covers money data.
