Ask Jarso is now on for every child by default, and a parent can turn it off at any time on the child's page. It's merged to `main` as #39 (8a5571c), all CI checks passed, and the branch is deleted.

- **How it works:** a child whose parent never touched the switch has Jarso on. Only a parent turning it off stops it, both in the app and in the database. Your demo child is now on automatically, so there's nothing to re-enable.
- **Parents are told:** the add-child form and both invite-approval paths show a short notice. It says Jarso is on, what it looks at, that messages go to Anthropic, and how to turn it off. The switch text, `/safety` and the landing copy say the same.

Before merging I fixed three things:
- **My e2e test:** it found the add-child notice twice, because Next keeps the previous page hidden in the page.
- **Invite approvals:** CodeRabbit spotted that approving a child-started invite never showed the notice.
- **Deploy guide:** CodeRabbit asked for a tighter note; it now says to keep `FEATURE_GUIDE` on in Production only while every family is a demo or test account.

CodeRabbit left one minor point I haven't changed: the notice says "sent to Anthropic" even with the fake provider, which only runs in local dev and e2e. Easy to tidy later.

Two things are still on you:
- **Legal review:** a default-on switch is weaker than the opt-in consent COPPA expects before an under-13's data goes to a third party. I noted it in ADR 0011 and the PR, so the review before real families has to confirm it or bring back an opt-in.
- **Production:** turn `FEATURE_GUIDE` off before the first real family signs up, and run the paid evals before switching it back on.

Ask Jarso is on the child's dashboard, not the parent's. Production is running the new version: the live landing page shows the new copy and the Jarso endpoint answers.

**Child (`/child`):** sign in as a child. Ask Jarso is the first card, right under "Hi, <name>!" and above the next-step card. It has suggested questions ("How much do I have?", "What did I spend this week?" and two more), a question box with an Ask button, and the replies inline. The first time, it shows a short "Before we start" card; tap "Got it" to get to the box.

**Parent (`/parent`):** there's no Ask Jarso window for parents yet, because we built it for children first. On each child's page (`/parent` → tap the child) there's a "Jarso, the AI helper" card with the on/off switch, which is on by default, and a "Read <name>'s chats" link. Each reply in a chat shows what Jarso looked at.

If the card doesn't appear on a child's Home, one of these is the reason:
- `FEATURE_GUIDE` isn't `true` in Vercel Production;
- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` isn't set there;
- the parent has turned Jarso off for that child.

**1. Error code docs** (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/debugging/error-codes, HTTP 200)
The table row reads: `email_address_invalid` | "Example and test domains are currently not supported. Use a different email address."
The page does not list which domains, TLDs, or checks are involved. Nothing about MX, DNS, syntax, or `.invalid`/`.test`.

**2. SMTP page** (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/auth-smtp, HTTP 200)
No section on email validation, blocked domains, disposable emails, or MX checks. The only address restriction is for the default SMTP service: "Supabase Auth will refuse to deliver messages to addresses that are not part of the project's team." That failure is `email_address_not_authorized` ("Email address not authorized."), a different code. The page also says custom SMTP "will send messages to all addresses."

**3. GoTrue source** (https://github.com/supabase/auth/blob/master/internal/api/mail.go, HTTP 200)
- Input check in `validateEmail`: "if err := checkmail.ValidateFormat(email); err != nil { ... "Unable to validate email address: %s" }". This returns `ErrorCodeValidationFailed` (`validation_failed`), not `email_address_invalid`. It runs in `adminGenerateLink` as well as in other paths.
- `email_address_invalid` comes from `sendEmail`. The mailer's return values are checked: `errors.Is(err, validateclient.ErrInvalidEmailAddress)`, `validateclient.ErrInvalidEmailFormat`, or `validateclient.ErrInvalidEmailDNS`. Each maps to `apierrors.NewBadRequestError(apierrors.ErrorCodeEmailAddressInvalid, "Email address %q is invalid", recipientEmail)`.
- So the code has a DNS-level check (`ErrInvalidEmailDNS`). The actual rules live in `internal/mailer/validateclient`, which I did not fetch. I did not guess that URL.

**Answers to your questions**
- What makes an address invalid: Syntax alone (`checkmail.ValidateFormat`) gives `validation_failed`, not `email_address_invalid`. The `email_address_invalid` path is a mailer-side validation that includes a DNS check. Its exact rules (MX vs A lookup, blocklist, TLD list) are unverified from these pages. The error text "Example and test domains" suggests a domain blocklist, but the list is not shown.
- Admin API `createUser`: Unverified. `mail.go` shows that `sendEmail` runs the mailer validation only when an email is actually sent. `adminGenerateLink` calls the syntax-only `validateEmail` first. I did not read `internal/api/admin.go`, so I cannot confirm whether `createUser` triggers `sendEmail` or the mailer validation.
- Config option to disable or relax it: None found in any of the three pages. No dashboard setting or GoTrue env var is named for this check. The only related knobs in the source are `External.Email.AuthorizedAddresses` (which gives `email_address_not_authorized`) and `Mailer.Autoconfirm`/SMTP settings. Neither is documented as disabling this check.
- `<uuid>@child.jarso.vercel.app` with no MX record: Unverified. It depends on whether `validateclient`'s DNS check is MX-based. If it is, this address would likely fail. If it only checks the host or A record, it would likely pass. I could not determine this from the fetched pages.

**Gaps and next steps**

Both source files were read. The DNS and blocklist rules are now known, but whether the check is enabled on hosted Supabase is still unverified.

Pages read (both HTTP 200 via raw.githubusercontent.com on the first try):
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/supabase/auth/master/internal/mailer/validateclient/validateclient.go
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/supabase/auth/master/internal/api/admin.go
Also relied on from the earlier read: https://github.com/supabase/auth/blob/master/internal/api/mail.go

**1. validateclient: what the DNS check does, and the blocklists**

It is off unless a config flag enables it. `New` wraps the mailer only when enabled:
"// New will return a Client that first calls an email validator before passing the mail along... If email validation is disabled then it returns the same Client passed in mc."
`isEnabled()` is `return ev.isExtendedEnabled() || ev.isServiceEnabled()`, where `isExtendedEnabled` is `ev.extended` and `isServiceEnabled` is `ev.serviceURL != ""`.
The flags come from `newEmailValidator(mc conf.MailerConfiguration)`: `mc.EmailValidationExtended`, `mc.EmailValidationServiceURL`, `mc.GetEmailValidationServiceHeaders()`, `mc.GetEmailValidationBlockedMXRecords()`.
Their defaults are in `internal/conf` (not fetched). Since `isEnabled()` is false when both are unset, the check is off by default unless the default in conf is true. Env var names are not shown here and I am not stating them.

The DNS check is `validateHost`:
- Single-label host: "if !strings.Contains(host, ".") { return ErrInvalidEmailDNS }"
- Allow list: `hostAllowList` (gmail.com., yahoo.com., hotmail.com., etc.) skips DNS entirely.
- MX first: `mxs, err := validateEmailResolver.LookupMX(ctx, host)`; if `!isHostNotFound(err)` it returns `ev.validateMXRecords(mxs, nil)`.
- A/AAAA fallback: `hosts, err := validateEmailResolver.LookupHost(ctx, host)`; if `!isHostNotFound(err)` it returns `ev.validateMXRecords(nil, hosts)`.
- Otherwise: "// No addrs or mx records were found / return ErrInvalidEmailDNS".
- `validateMXRecords` only rejects hosts in the configured blocked-MX set, returning `ErrInvalidEmailMX`. It does not reject ordinary hosts.
- `isHostNotFound` returns true only for a definite DNS not-found error (non-temporary, non-timeout). Timeouts or other errors are treated as valid: "We will be unable to determine with absolute certainy the email was invalid so we will err on the side of caution and return nil."
- Note the comment: "if an empty list of MX's are returned the host should be treated as the MX" (so no MX with no error is treated as valid).

Static checks (`validateStatic`, which runs only when extended is on):
- `invalidEmailMap`: [email redacted], [email redacted], [email redacted], [email redacted].
- `invalidHostSuffixes` (RFC 2606): ".test", ".example", ".invalid", ".local", ".localhost". Matched with `strings.HasSuffix(host, ...)`, returning `ErrInvalidEmailDNS`.
- `invalidHostMap`: "test", "example", "invalid", "local", "localhost", "test.com", "example.com", "example.net", "example.org", "gamil.com", "gamai.com", "anonymous.com", "email.com". Matched on the exact host.
- Gmail: `validateProviders` rejects gmail.com local parts shorter than 6 characters.

Error values: `ErrInvalidEmailAddress = errors.New("invalid_email_address")`, `ErrInvalidEmailFormat = errors.New("invalid_email_format")`, `ErrInvalidEmailDNS = errors.New("invalid_email_dns")`, `ErrInvalidEmailMX = errors.New("invalid_email_mx")`.

Optional remote service: if `serviceURL` is set, it POSTs `{"email": ...}` and rejects only when `"valid"` is false. Errors are ignored.

**2. admin.go: adminUserCreate**

Quote: "if params.Email == \"\" && params.Phone == \"\" { return ...\"Cannot create a user without either an email or phone\" }"
Email branch: `params.Email, err = a.validateEmail(params.Email)`, then `models.IsDuplicatedEmail(...)`.
`validateEmail` (in mail.go) is syntax only: `checkmail.ValidateFormat(email)`, returning `validation_failed`. It does not call validateclient.
Phone branch: `validatePhone(params.Phone)`, then duplicate check.
`email_confirm` branch:
  "if params.EmailConfirm { if terr := user.Confirm(tx); terr != nil { return terr } }"
No `sendEmail`, no mailer call, no validateclient call anywhere in `adminUserCreate`. The function ends with `return sendJSON(w, http.StatusOK, user)`.
So for `createUser`, the `email_address_invalid` path never runs, with or without `email_confirm`. The only email check is the syntax one in `validateEmail`.

**3. Verdicts (assuming the extended check is on, which is unverified for hosted)**
- `<uuid>@child.jarso.invalid`: fails through `validateStatic`, host suffix ".invalid" returns `ErrInvalidEmailDNS`, and `sendEmail` maps it to `email_address_invalid`. Via `createUser`: it is created with no error, because validateclient never runs there. Note the syntax check does not reject ".invalid" (it is a valid format).
- `<uuid>@jarso.vercel.app` (resolves, no MX): passes. `LookupMX` returns no records with no not-found error, or a not-found error that falls through to `LookupHost`, which resolves. `validateMXRecords` then finds no blocked MX. Passes as long as the host is not in the blocked-MX config. (Exact MX-empty behavior of Go's resolver not separately verified.)
- `<uuid>@child.jarso.vercel.app` (does not resolve): `LookupMX` and `LookupHost` both return a definite not-found error, so `ErrInvalidEmailDNS`, and `sendEmail` maps it to `email_address_invalid`. Caveat: if the name is covered by a wildcard record it resolves and passes. Timeouts are treated as pass. Via `createUser`: created with no error.

**4. createUser with no email / phone only, and generateLink magiclink**
- No email and no phone: rejected. Quote: "Cannot create a user without either an email or phone" (400, `validation_failed`).
- Phone only: allowed. The email branch is skipped, and the phone branch runs `validatePhone` and the duplicate check. The `providers` list is `[PhoneProvider]`, and `providers[0]` becomes the provider.
- generateLink magiclink (`adminGenerateLink` in mail.go): `params.Email, err = a.validateEmail(params.Email)` runs first. Empty email returns `apierrors.NewBadRequestError(apierrors.ErrorCodeValidationFailed, "An email address is required")`. So a phone-only user cannot be targeted by generate_link magiclink, and a user with no email cannot be targeted at all.

**Remaining unverified**
- Whether extended or service validation is enabled on hosted Supabase, and the defaults for `EmailValidationExtended` and related fields (in `internal/conf`, not fetched).
- Exact env var or dashboard names for those flags. None are shown in these files.
- Whether `checkmail.ValidateFormat` (in `github.com/badoux/checkmail`) is strictly syntax only. This is from the library, not fetched.
- Go's resolver behavior for an existing domain with no MX (NODATA vs NXDOMAIN).
- Wildcard DNS on vercel.app (affects the child-subdomain case).
- `validatePhone` and the phone-only magiclink path in `internal/conf`/other files, not read.
- `internal/conf` (where defaults are set) and `validateSignupParams` (used by generateLink for unknown emails) were not fetched.

All three pages loaded (HTTP 200). The docs do not address Next.js server actions anywhere, and they do not say whether the CLI trims trailing newlines.

## 1. Sensitive / Secret variables and runtime availability

Source: https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables/sensitive-environment-variables (last updated 2026-08-28)

- The page no longer uses "sensitive" as the main term. It is titled "Config and Secret environment variables", and it says: "**Secret** values are write-only after saving. Use them for passwords, API keys, and tokens. Existing [Sensitive environment variables] continue to work as Secrets."
- The page makes no statement about Serverless Functions, the Node.js runtime, or request-time access for Secrets.
- The page makes no statement about build-step access for Secrets. It only covers build log redaction: "During builds, if a Secret environment variable value is 32 characters or longer and appears in build logs, Vercel replaces the value with `[REDACTED]`."
- The general page, https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables, does state the runtime and build access: "Your source code can read these values to change behavior during the [Build Step](/docs/builds/configure-a-build) or during [Function](/docs/functions) execution." It does not separate Secret from Config there.
- The page states: "> **💡 Note:** Secrets are allowed in Development. This reverses the earlier rule that limited Sensitive environment variables to Production and Preview."
- The legacy policy section has older wording: the old policy required variables to be stored as Sensitive, "decryptable only during deployments." That is marked deprecated, so do not treat it as current behavior.

## 2. `vercel env add` (https://vercel.com/docs/cli/env)

**Stdin:** The page lists these usages:
- `vercel env add [name] [environment] < [file]`: "Using the `vercel env` command to add an Environment Variable to a Vercel Project using a local file's content as the value."
- `echo [value] | vercel env add [name] [environment]`: "Using the `echo` command to generate the value of the Environment Variable and piping that value into the `vercel env add` command. Warning: this will save the value in bash history, so this is not recommended for secrets."
- `cat ~/.npmrc | vercel env update NPM_RC preview`: "Update an Environment Variable value from stdin."

`printf` is not mentioned, but piping is documented. The page also says: "In an interactive terminal, the CLI asks `Environment Variable type?` unless you pass a type flag, `--yes`, `--value`, or a value through stdin."

**Trailing newline:** The page does not say whether a trailing newline is trimmed. The only related documented point is the echo warning above, which is about bash history, not whitespace.

**`--sensitive`:**
- "The `--sensitive` and `--no-sensitive` flags map to Secret and Config. `--visibility` is a deprecated alias for `--type`."
- Example: `vercel env add API_KEY production --value "sk_live_..." --sensitive --yes` ("`--sensitive` infers `type: secret`.")
- "Using `--sensitive` or `--no-sensitive` with a conflicting `--type` returns an error."
- Default type table: Production `secret`, Preview `secret`, Development `config`, Custom environments `secret`. So `printf 'x' | vercel env add NAME production` creates a Secret unless you add `--no-sensitive` or `--type config`.
- "Variables with a public framework prefix, such as `NEXT_PUBLIC_`, are stored as Config."

**`--force`:** "The `--force` option overwrites an existing environment variable of the same target without prompting for confirmation." Example: `vercel env add API_TOKEN production --force`.

## 3. Changes apply only to new deployments

Source: https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables (last updated 2026-09-17)

- "Any change you make to environment variables are not applied to previous deployments, they only apply to new deployments."
- Environment docs: "When selected, the Environment Variable will be applied to your next Production Deployment." and for Preview: "The Environment Variable is applied to your next Preview Deployment."
- The integrations section of the sensitive page says: "Redeploy your projects to use the new values."
- For local files, the CLI page says: "After updating environment variables on Vercel (through the dashboard, `vercel env add`, or `vercel env rm`), you will have to run `vercel env pull <file>` again to get the updated values."

The docs do not use the exact phrase "existing deployment keeps its old values". The quoted sentence above is the closest statement and implies it.

## 4. Limitations for server actions or `--force`

No documented limitation exists for Next.js server actions. No documented limitation exists for `--force`, beyond its prompt-skipping behavior.

Documented restrictions that do apply:
- "You cannot edit the key of a Secret after it is saved."
- "For **Secret** variables, the current value is hidden. Provide a new value to rotate the secret. You cannot convert a saved Secret to Config in place."
- "Environment variables created by [integrations] can't be changed to Secret from the environment variables list."
- Build log redaction applies only to Secret values of 32 characters or longer, as quoted in section 1.

## Pages read

- https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables/sensitive-environment-variables
- https://vercel.com/docs/cli/env
- https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables

Security audit: branch feat/family-settings-unlink (uncommitted vs main 908a260), read-only.

Pre-work scan (per standing rules): no lines >500 chars, no eval/new Function/child_process/blockchain URLs, no .gitignore change. Diff, untracked files and the .claude-logs diff contain no credential-shaped strings and no non-noreply/non-example.com emails. No new credential type is introduced, so scripts/check-secrets.sh, .gitleaks.toml and REDACTIONS in .claude/hooks/extract-log.py remain sufficient as they are.

No Critical or High findings.

## Medium

**M1. Removal has no retry path: a failed session revoke leaves a removed child with live refresh tokens and a live identity that the parent can never finish removing.**
- /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/family/server/service.ts:306-311 and /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261029100000_family_settings.sql:41 (`is_parent_of` gate), test codified at /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/supabase/tests/database/family_settings.test.sql:117 ("removing twice is refused").
- Scenario: `remove_child` commits (membership removed, PIN gone), then `revokeUserSessions` throws (admin client/env issue); the action returns an error, the child has vanished from the parent's list, and any retry is refused with JA403 because `is_parent_of` now returns false, so the child's refresh tokens keep renewing a `role=child` session indefinitely and the auth user is never deleted. Separately, `deleteChildAuthUser` (/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/family/server/child-auth.ts:36-39) only logs on failure, so the parent is told "Their account is deleted" (/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/family/components/remove-child-dialog.tsx:48-49) while the auth user and the profile row with its nickname persist, and the log line says "orphan child auth user", which is misleading for this path.
- Fix: make `remove_child` idempotent for the child's own family (accept a `status = 'removed'` row whose `family_id` is the caller's approved family; return the same deletable flag), so `removeChildAction` can be re-run until revoke + delete succeed; have the removal path use a throwing variant of the auth delete (keep the swallow-and-log behaviour only for the addChild cleanup path) and log it with a distinct message; update the pgTAP "removing twice" expectation accordingly.

**M2. TOCTOU between the parent check and the row lock in `rename_child` and `remove_child`: neither re-checks `status = 'approved'` after acquiring `for update`.**
- /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261029100000_family_settings.sql:41-50 (remove) and :100-118 (rename).
- Scenario: concurrent remove + rename from the same parent (two tabs): rename passes `is_parent_of`, waits on the row lock, then writes `nickname_key` back onto the now-`removed` row and emits a `child.renamed` audit row after `child.removed`; concurrent double-remove writes two `child.removed` audit rows and calls the auth delete twice. Today the identity delete cascades the membership row almost immediately so impact is cosmetic, but the moment `remove_child` returns false (money history, per ADR 0007) the removed row keeps a `nickname_key` and the partial unique index (`role = 'child'` only, /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261028182342_child_accounts.sql:14-16) blocks that nickname for the family permanently.
- Fix: select `fm.status` into a variable in both `for update` queries and `raise ... errcode = 'JA403'` when `not found or v_status <> 'approved'`.

## Low

**L1. Child data lingers after removal despite the data-minimisation framing in ADR 0007.**
- Audit rows keep the nickname forever: `child.removed` (/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261029100000_family_settings.sql:68) and `child.renamed` from/to (:128); `profiles.nickname` is not cleared by `remove_child` (matters whenever the identity is kept or the delete fails); and the invitation-expiry statement at :62-65 is a no-op because `invitations.child_id` is only ever set together with `status = 'accepted'` in `register_child`, so no pending invitation can match. `child_pin_attempts`, `child_credentials` and usage_counters are handled correctly (deleted / keyed by hashes, not child id).
- Fix: null `profiles.nickname` in `remove_child`; either drop the invitation statement or key it on something that can match; consider storing only `subject_id` in removal audit details or adding a retention job for audit rows of deleted subjects.

**L2. `toAppError` now logs Postgres `details`/`hint` for unmapped codes.**
- /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/lib/db-error.ts:28-34.
- Verified: every `raise` in the three new functions uses a constant message, mapped SQLSTATEs short-circuit before the log, both `unique_violation` paths that could include a nickname are caught and re-raised as JA409, and current unique/FK keys are UUIDs or hashes. Residual risk only: Postgres DETAIL for 23505/23503 embeds key values, so a future unique index on an email or free-text column would log PII. Fix: log `code` and `message` only, or allow-list `details` per SQLSTATE class.

**L3. No rate limit on the settings mutations.**
- /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/family/server/actions.ts:107-129. Each rename/timezone call writes an audit row and `update_family_timezone` scans `pg_timezone_names`; an authenticated parent can grow `audit_log` without bound. Acceptable at ~60 users; a cheap `hitRateLimit` keyed on `settings:user:<id>` would close it.

## Verified OK (no finding)
- IDOR: both the action (`assertParentOf`) and the function (`is_parent_of`, which requires caller approved parent in the same family and the target an approved child) gate remove/rename; a child calling on themselves and a parent of another family get JA403 (pgTAP covers both). `update_family_timezone` resolves the family from `auth.uid()`, never from input.
- Grants: all three functions are `security definer`, `set search_path = ''`, revoked from `public, anon`, granted only to `authenticated` (migration :184-193).
- Removed child cannot sign in: `verify_child_pin` requires `fm.status = 'approved'` and credentials are deleted. Live-session window: refresh tokens die with `revoke_user_sessions`; a locally-verified access token lives up to 1 h as already documented for PIN reset, and with membership gone RLS exposes only the child's own profile row.
- Concurrent rename vs register of the same nickname is serialised by the unique index and mapped to JA409.
- CSRF/validation: Server Actions (Next origin check), hidden `childId` validated as `z.uuid()`, nickname/timezone validated by Zod and again in Postgres.
- Profile path: `updateProfileSchema` carries only `fullName`/`avatarUrl`; column grant is `full_name, avatar_url`; the update policy at /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261029013254_harden_onboarding.sql:415-418 restricts updates to `role = 'parent'`, so a child session cannot use `updateProfileAction` to store a name.
- Pages: `/settings`, `/parent/settings`, `/child/settings` call `requireUser`/`requireRole` inside `<Suspense>`; `/settings` added to `protectedRoutePrefixes`. The parent page renders only the parent's own email and family code; `ChildSummary` is id + nickname. No `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; nicknames render as JSX text and sonner text.
- Hand-edited /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/types/database.types.ts matches the migration signatures (`remove_child` returns boolean; `removed_at`/`removed_by` columns and FK); recommend regenerating with `pnpm db:types` before merge to rule out drift.

Supabase GitHub integration: deploy migrations to production on merge, without GitHub Actions

Pages read (all HTTP 200):
- https://supabase.com/docs/guides/deployment/branching/github-integration
- https://supabase.com/docs/guides/deployment/branching
- https://supabase.com/docs/guides/deployment/database-migrations
- https://supabase.com/pricing
- https://supabase.com/docs/guides/deployment/branching/configuration (followed link, for [remotes])
- https://supabase.com/docs/guides/deployment/branching/troubleshooting (followed link, for status and failures)

Quotes are kept under 125 characters each. Items marked UNVERIFIED were not confirmed by the pages.

## 1. Plans and price

- Free plan: the pricing table row reads "Branching | Not included" for Free, and "$0.01344 per branch, per hour" for Pro and Team (source: pricing page).
- The pricing FAQ says: "Branching, available on the Pro Plan and above, to run a separate development environment".
- The Branching docs say: "You can deploy directly from GitHub on any plan — connect your repository and changes pushed to `main` are automatically deployed to your project."
- So the docs say GitHub deploy-on-merge works on any plan, and paid Branching adds preview branches per PR. These two statements are not fully reconciled on the pages. Confirm in the dashboard on a Free project (Project Settings > Integrations) that "Deploy to production" can be enabled. This is UNVERIFIED.
- Price unit for Branching: per branch, per hour, at $0.01344 (the pricing page has no per-branch-hour compute breakdown beyond that). Pro starts at $25/month. Pro and Team include $10/month of compute credits.

## 2. What runs on push or merge to the production branch

- Setting: "Enable the **Deploy to production** option in your GitHub Integration configuration to automatically deploy changes when you push or merge to production branch." So the option must be enabled.
- What is deployed, per github-integration page:
  - "New migrations are applied"
  - "Edge Functions declared in `config.toml` are deployed"
  - "Storage buckets declared in `config.toml` are deployed"
  - "All other configurations, including API, Auth, and seed files, are ignored by default."
- So by default, production gets migrations, plus functions and buckets declared in config.toml. Seed and auth config are not applied.
- Vault: the Branching overview's Migrate step says it "Applies pending database migrations and vault secrets to your branch". The production list above does not mention vault. Treat vault on production as UNVERIFIED.
- Workflow order (branching page): Clone, Pull, Health, Configure, Migrate, Seed, Deploy. Configure reads config.toml, Migrate runs pending migrations, Seed runs seed files, Deploy deploys functions and function secrets.
- Config.toml on production: the github-integration page says the Configure step "compares the `[db]`, `[api]`, `[auth]`, `[storage]`, and `[experimental]` sections" with the branch and updates differences. On a persistent branch, "including your production branch, the step runs only when a `[remotes]` block names that branch's project ID." This conflicts with the "ignored by default" line above. The `[remotes]` condition is the way to enable it. Exact production behavior is UNVERIFIED without testing.
- Production branch name and the Supabase directory path are set in the Integrations page. The working directory is "the path from your repository root to the directory that contains the `supabase/` folder. Enter `.` when `supabase/` is at the repository root."

## 3. Setup needed

- Dashboard: "Project Settings > Integrations", then "Under **GitHub Integration**, click **Authorize GitHub**." On GitHub, "Click **Authorize Supabase**." Then choose a repository, set "**Working directory**", and click "**Enable integration**."
- The pages describe authorizing the Supabase GitHub app on the repository. They do not list the exact GitHub permission scopes. UNVERIFIED.
- Prerequisite: `supabase init`, then `supabase db pull --db-url <session pooler string>`, then commit the `supabase` folder.
- The production branch name is the GitHub branch linked to production. Change it on the Integrations page (troubleshooting page).

## 4. Status reporting and failures

- PR comment: "A comment is added to your PR with the deployment status of your preview branch." This applies to PRs.
- Check name: the example workflow waits for a check named "Supabase Preview". The page does not explicitly say that check is always reported on pushes to production. UNVERIFIED.
- Step statuses (troubleshooting): `CREATED`, `RUNNING`, `EXITED` (success), `PAUSED` (warning or skipped on purpose, later steps still run), `DEAD` (failed, dependent steps don't run).
- "A `DEAD` `migrate` step stops the `seed` and `deploy` steps." Health failure is only a warning.
- Logs: Dashboard, "Manage Branches", click the branch, "View logs". The Management API can be polled for branch status.
- Recommended: "We highly recommend turning on a 'required check' for the Supabase integration." This is a GitHub branch protection setting.
- Recommended: subscribe to email notifications on the branch.
- Note: the GitHub Action example in the docs is a check-waiter for PRs (it watches `pull_request`). It is not needed to deploy.
- A failed migration leaves production unchanged for that migration. The migrations run in a single transaction each (see caveats). The exact retry behavior on a failed production push (does the next push retry it?) is UNVERIFIED.

## 5. Caveats

- Each migration file runs in one transaction. "Branching runs each migration file inside a single transaction and does not honor the `-- pg-delta: transaction=false` directive." Consequence: "`create index concurrently`" fails under branching but works with `supabase db push`.
- Migration ordering: "A migration file with a timestamp older than the latest applied migration still runs and the step finishes with a warning." Also: "A version in the branch's migration history with no matching file fails the step."
- Declarative schemas: files in `supabase/schemas/` are not applied by branching. Run `supabase db schema declarative sync` and commit the generated migration.
- Stop branching from running migrations: set `enabled = false` under `[db.migrations]`.
- Seed data: "Data changes in your seed files are not merged to production." Seeds are not applied to production by default.
- Secrets: production edge function secrets come from `[edge_runtime.secrets]` and encrypted `.env` files. Auth secrets on new branches get placeholders: "Auth secrets, such as OAuth provider secrets and SMTP passwords, get placeholder values instead."
- `[remotes.*]` syntax from the configuration page:
  ```
  [remotes.production]
  project_id = "prod-project-ref"
  ```
  "Each remote configuration must reference an existing project ID." If the project ID is wrong or no remote is declared, "the configuration step is skipped." Get the ID with `supabase --experimental branches list`.
- The docs say you "cannot change which project branch serves as the production branch." Only the linked GitHub branch can be changed.
- Migration history: schema changes made directly in the remote dashboard "bypasses the migration history and will cause `db push` to fail with sync errors." Repair with `supabase migration repair`. Only one person should run `db push` at a time.
- Schema drift: merging a preview into production creates drift for other open preview branches. Rebase and renumber as needed.
- Timestamp conflicts: migration files need unique, correctly ordered timestamps.
- Email and SMTP: the pricing page shows "Custom SMTP server | Included" on all plans, and "Remove Supabase branding from emails | Not included" on Free. It gives no SMTP send-rate limits, and the pages read did not cover SMTP limits. Email template limits and free-tier SMTP caps are UNVERIFIED.
- The pricing page says Free projects "are paused after 1 week of inactivity." Paid projects "are not paused for inactivity." Relevant if production were on Free.

## Unverified

- Whether "Deploy to production" works on the Free plan (docs say "any plan", pricing table says Branching is not included on Free).
- Whether vault secrets are applied to production on push.
- Exact effect of config.toml on production without a `[remotes]` block.
- GitHub permission scopes requested by the Supabase app.
- Whether the "Supabase Preview" check is reported on production pushes.
- Retry behavior after a failed production deploy.
- SMTP send limits.

Not fetched: https://supabase.com/docs/guides/deployment/branching/dashboard and https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/manage-your-usage/branching (linked from the config page, not needed for these questions).

Review of the uncommitted work on feat/parent-profile-completion (base 23204d1), done read-only.

I scanned every changed and untracked file for obfuscated code first: no lines over 500 characters, and no `eval`, `new Function`, `child_process`, `windowsHide` or RPC URLs. The session-log diff has no credential-shaped strings.

**The tree changed while I was reviewing.** `src/app/(app)/invite/page.tsx` and `docs/auth.md` were modified mid-review. The invite change fixes the worst problem I found (below). I re-checked that the other files were the same as when I read them.

**Fixed during the review.** Before the invite edit, `/invite` crashed for a parent who has a family but no profile — which is every existing parent once this ships. `listMyInvitations` and `findInvitation` call `assertRole("parent")` while the page renders, so they threw FORBIDDEN. The only error boundary is `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/app/error.tsx`, which sits above the `(app)` layout, so the whole shell, dialog included, became "Something went wrong". The new check at `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/app/(app)/invite/page.tsx:47-52` is correct and covers the list, link, code and id cases. Its copy, "Then this invitation will open.", reads oddly on plain `/invite`, which shows a list rather than one invitation. No e2e covers this case.

## Blocking
None.

## Should fix

1. **Code that ships before the migration takes down every parent page.**
   - Docs: `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/docs/database.md:30` says migrations must be expand/contract because the Supabase integration and the Vercel build run side by side. This PR ships the table and the code that requires it in one squash.
   - Gate: if Vercel goes live first, the query at `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/profiles/server/repository.ts:46` throws INTERNAL (PostgREST missing-table error, not mapped in `db-error.ts`). It throws inside `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/app/(app)/profile-gate.tsx:14`, so the root error boundary replaces the whole `(app)` shell on every parent page.
   - Actions: every parent action fails with "Failed to load your details" because it goes through `assertRole`.
   - Children are not affected: `currentRole()` returns before the table is queried.
   - Lasting effect: the same thing happens on any later transient database error, since every parent page now runs this query in the layout.
   - Fix:
     - Merge the migration on its own first and confirm the Supabase deploy succeeded, then merge the code.
     - Make the gate fail open: try/catch around `isParentProfileComplete`, `logger.warn`, return null. This is safe because `assertRole` still fails closed on the server.

2. **Date of birth is wiped after any failed submit.**
   - Where: `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/profiles/components/parent-details-form.tsx:62-73`. The date field is uncontrolled (`defaultValue`), and React clears uncontrolled fields after a form action.
   - Effect: a parent who only forgot gender, or hits a server error, has to re-enter their date. The repo already handles this elsewhere — see the comment at `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/family/components/add-child-form.tsx:13`. The e2e helper always refills the date, so tests never notice.
   - Fix: `const [dob, setDob] = useState(defaults?.dateOfBirth ?? "")` and pass `value`/`onChange` to the field.

3. **Duplicate element ids when a parent without a profile opens `/parent/settings`.**
   - Where: `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/app/(app)/parent/settings/page.tsx:68-74` always renders `ParentDetailsForm`, and the gate renders a second copy in the dialog. Both use fixed ids (`dateOfBirth` via `Field` `id={name}`, plus `country`, `gender`, `country-error`, `gender-error`).
   - Effect: the dialog is portaled to the end of `<body>`, so its labels point at the inputs behind the modal. Its date input and comboboxes lose their accessible names, and clicking a label focuses a hidden control. You can reach this state with a direct URL or a reload for any existing parent.
   - Fix: render the "Your details" section only when `details` is non-null (the dialog handles first completion), or prefix the ids with `useId()`.

## Nit
- **Duplicate auth check:** `assertUser()` runs in both `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/profiles/server/actions.ts:18` and `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/profiles/server/service.ts:44`. Keep one, ideally the `updateProfile` pattern where the action passes `user.id`.
- **Unused export:** `PROFILE_INCOMPLETE` is exported at `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/family/server/dal.ts:48` but not from the barrel, and the invite page repeats the literal. Re-export and reuse it, or drop the `export`.
- **Same table read twice:** the settings page reads `parent_details` twice per request (`getParentDetails` plus the gate's `isParentProfileComplete`). Wrap `getParentDetails` in `cache()` and derive `isParentProfileComplete` from it.
- **Silent "incomplete":** `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/profiles/server/repository.ts:47` treats a saved row with a country outside the app's list as incomplete. The database accepts any `[A-Z]{2}` through a direct RPC call. It heals itself on resubmit, but add a `logger.warn` so it isn't invisible.
- **Copy for existing parents:** the dialog text at `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/profiles/components/profile-completion-dialog.tsx:26` says "before you set up your family", which is wrong for parents who already have one. Use neutral wording.
- **Unneeded `useMemo`:** the country list and items at `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/profiles/components/parent-details-form.tsx:42-52` are static; module-level constants would be simpler.
- **Stale test counts:** `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/docs/fr-audit.md:23` still says "pgTAP 195, e2e 14"; it is now 219 and 16.
- **Hand-edited types:** `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/types/database.types.ts` matches the migration (columns, the one-to-one key, argument names, `Returns: undefined`, alphabetical order). Rule 7 still says to regenerate, so run `pnpm db:types` before committing.

## Answers to your specific questions
- **Cache Components:** fine.
  - The gate reads cookies inside its own Suspense, and nothing is awaited at the top of the layout.
  - Next's `io()` docs say no `io()` is needed when a request-time API comes first. Both places that use `new Date()` (`latestAdultBirthDate()`) are already behind one: the gate after `getCurrentUser`, settings after `requireRole`. So nothing ends up in the prerendered page.
  - The dialog is portaled, so it is not rendered on the server at all. The settings `max` uses UTC on both sides, so the only possible hydration difference is right at UTC midnight.
- **Imports:**
  - No cycle: `family/server/dal` imports `@/features/profiles/server`, and profiles never imports family. Putting `ProfileGate` in `app/` is what keeps it that way.
  - The client components reach the server only through `../server/actions` (`"use server"`), the same as `display-name-form`. Nothing `server-only` is reachable from client code.
- **`assertRole` coverage:**
  - Every parent mutation is gated:
    - `createFamilyAction`, `addChildAction`, `removeChildAction` and `updateFamilySettingsAction` call `assertRole` directly.
    - `acceptInvitationAction` and `acceptListedInvitationAction` go through `requireFamilyId` / `findInvitation`.
    - `setChildPinAction` and `renameChildAction` go through `assertParentOf`.
  - Not gated, as intended: `completeParentProfileAction` (only `assertUser`, confirmed), `updateProfileAction` and `PATCH /api/v1/me` (display name, documented exception in architecture.md 7.1a), and `signOut`.
  - The only thing that broke was render-time reads on `/invite`, now fixed.
- **Base UI:**
  - Controlled `open` with a no-op `onOpenChange` really cannot be dismissed. Escape calls `onOpenChange`, which is ignored, and AlertDialog ignores outside clicks.
  - The Select popup is portaled into the dialog's own portal node (`FloatingPortal.js:64`). The dialog's focus manager skips nested portals when it hides the page (`FloatingFocusManager.js:340-352`), so the options stay usable.
  - The `SelectValue` `placeholder` prop is valid (`SelectValue.d.ts:37`), and so is a record passed as `items`.
- **Zod 4.6.7:** `z.iso.date({ error }).refine(fn, "msg")` is valid. Format errors don't stop later checks (`checks.js:354`), so an empty date produces three errors. That's harmless, because `FieldError` only shows the first.
- **pgTAP:** 24 assertions, matching `plan(24)`. Helper usage and the `set local role anon` / `reset role` sequence match `family_core.test.sql:98-100`. The completed_at test is sound: it backdates to 2020 as superuser, so the in-transaction `now()` can't hide a regression.
- **e2e:**
  - The selectors match the real labels and roles. The combobox naming is the same pattern as Timezone, which already passes.
  - The modal sets `aria-hidden` on the page behind it, so `toHaveCount(0)` for "Create family" is a valid check.
  - "Sign out" is scoped to the dialog, and the header button is hidden behind the modal anyway.
  - Every parent sign-up path completes the profile. `email-confirm.spec.ts` only checks the URL, so it still passes.

Verdict: no blocking defects. Fix the three Should-fix items (including the merge order) before shipping to a production database with real parents.

## Security and privacy audit: feat/parent-profile-completion (uncommitted, base main 23204d1)

No Critical or High findings. Only the owner can read a parent's details, the 18+, country and gender rules hold, and nothing leaks into logs, `audit_log` or other pages. The issues below are about availability and keeping the server-side block and its docs consistent.

This was a read-only review; I ran no pnpm, node or supabase commands. The required obfuscation scan was clean: the only lines over 500 characters are Tailwind class strings in unchanged shadcn files (`src/components/ui/{dropdown-menu,select,sheet}.tsx`). There is no eval, `new Function`, `child_process` in config files, RPC URLs, `.bat` files or `.gitignore` change.

### Medium

**M1. Code deployed before the migration locks out every parent (fails closed, no data exposed).**
- **Where:** `src/features/profiles/server/repository.ts:46`, `src/app/(app)/profile-gate.tsx:14`, `src/features/family/server/dal.ts:58`.
- **Risk:** If the app ships before `parent_details` exists, the lookup fails and the gate throws an INTERNAL error. The nearest error boundary is `src/app/error.tsx`, which sits above the `(app)` layout, so every parent page shows "Something went wrong" with no dialog. Every `assertRole("parent")` action fails too.
- **This already applies to this PR's preview:** previews use the production Supabase project (`docs/deploy.md:22`), which does not have the table yet.
- **On merge:** Vercel and the Supabase GitHub integration deploy independently (`docs/deploy.md:41`, `docs/database.md:26`). A failed or slow migration means a full parent outage.
- **Children are unaffected:** the gate returns early for them.
- **Fix:** Merge the migration first in its own PR, or confirm in the Supabase dashboard that the integration applied it before promoting the Vercel build. Keep failing closed: do not treat "table not found" as a complete profile.

### Low

**L1. The block is enforced in the Next.js server but not in the database.**
- **Where:** grants on `create_family` (`supabase/migrations/20261028181734_family_core.sql:278`), `remove_child`, `rename_child` and `update_family_timezone` (`20261029100000_family_settings.sql:222`), and `set_child_pin` (`20261028182342_child_accounts.sql:314`).
- **Risk:** A parent who hasn't completed their profile can call these functions directly through the Supabase API with their own session and the public publishable key, skipping `assertRole`. `register_child` is still effectively gated, because it needs a child identity that only the server creates.
- **Impact is small:** the parent is already authorised for these actions, and the 18+ attestation is self-reported anyway.
- **Docs overclaim:** `docs/architecture.md:460` says the block "is enforced on the server", and ADR 0008:14 says it "holds even if the dialog is bypassed".
- **Fix:** Add a one-line `exists (select 1 from public.parent_details where profile_id = auth.uid())` check to the parent definer functions now. Otherwise, reword both docs to "the app server refuses…" and keep the ADR's plan to add the database check with the money functions.

**L2. Some parent mutations skip the gate, and the docs don't mention it.**
- **Where:** `updateProfileAction` (`src/features/profiles/server/actions.ts:9-14`) and `PATCH /api/v1/me` (`src/app/api/v1/me/route.ts:11-15`) use `assertUser`, not `assertRole`.
- **Risk:** An incomplete parent can still change their display name or avatar, which contradicts "refuses every parent action" in ADR 0008 and REQUIREMENTS FR13. Impact is negligible because it only touches their own row.
- **Fix:** List the intended exceptions (own display name, sign-out, the completion action) in ADR 0008. Also update `docs/auth.md:11`, which describes `assertRole` without the profile check and is the reference future actions will copy.

**L3. Invitation links break for an existing parent who hasn't completed the profile (availability, not confidentiality).**
- **Where:** `src/app/(app)/invite/page.tsx:63`, `:75` and `:96` call `listMyInvitations`, `findInvitation` and `findMyInvitation` while rendering. All go through `assertRole("parent")`.
- **Risk:** A parent who already has a family opens a child's invitation email and gets the root error page instead of the completion dialog. "Try again" just repeats the error.
- **Fix:** On `/invite`, check `isParentProfileComplete` first, or catch the "Finish your profile first." error and render a notice, so the layout's dialog stays mounted.

### Verified OK (what you asked about)

- **Who can read the details:**
  - **Direct table reads:** RLS allows owner-only select (`supabase/migrations/20261029120000_parent_details.sql:34-37`), and anon has no access at all (`:31`).
  - **Embedding from `profiles`:** filtered by the same policy.
  - **Other read paths:** no view or other function reads `parent_details`, and it is not in a realtime publication.
  - **Tests:** pgTAP covers the child, the other parent and their own row.
- **Bypassing the rules:**
  - **Direct writes:** insert, update, delete and truncate are revoked from `authenticated` (`:32`), and pgTAP checks direct insert and update return 42501.
  - **The function:** it is security definer with `search_path = ''` and fully qualified names, and writes only the caller's own row. The upsert path re-runs every check, null arguments are rejected explicitly (`:65-77`), and anon and public lose execute rights (`:91`).
  - **The country list:** the database accepts any two capital letters, but an unlisted code makes `findParentDetails` return null (`repository.ts:47`), so the gate fails closed.
- **Calling the function:**
  - **Children:** get JA403 from the parent-role check (`:61-63`). `profiles.role` cannot be edited by users.
  - **Signed-out callers:** get JA401 from the function, and the action refuses them first with `assertUser`.
  - **Completion while blocked:** the completion action is deliberately reachable (`actions.ts:17-21`).
- **PII in logs:** The new raise messages are fixed strings with no user values. `toAppError` logs only the code and database message, never the detail. Zod rejects malformed dates before Postgres can echo the input in an error. No new log line includes date of birth, country or gender.
- **audit_log:** never written by this feature.
- **Client exposure:** the date of birth reaches only the owner's `/parent/settings` page (`page.tsx:29,68`). The dialog gets no defaults, and `/api/v1/me` does not include the details.
- **Public repo:** tests and fixtures use synthetic values only (example.com addresses, "Profile Parent", 1985-06-15). The diff, new files and the `.claude-logs` diff contain no credential-shaped strings and no real emails (only a GitHub noreply address). No new credential type is introduced, so `scripts/check-secrets.sh`, `.gitleaks.toml` and the REDACTIONS list need no change.

### Privacy note (product decision, not a vulnerability)

The app stores the full date of birth with no retention limit, although only the 18+ result is used. Gender is collected for pilot analytics, and the details are deleted only with the account. Make sure the privacy notice covers this, or consider keeping just the year of birth after the 18+ check.

time: 2026-10-29 20:09
chars: 7341

You are authoring the learning content for Jarso, a pocket-money learning app for children aged 11 to 14 (repo: /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso, branch feat/learning-journey). The engine, schema and mission 1 already exist. Your job is to write the remaining content as JSON files that pass the Zod schema. Content quality matters: real children will read every word, and the product owner reviews it in the PR.

## Read first (all of them, fully)
- `src/features/learning/schemas.ts`: the exact JSON shapes and cross-field rules (bodyProblems, checkSteps, missionSchema, topicSchema, reviewPoolSchema, checkInSchema). Every file you write must satisfy them.
- `src/features/learning/skills.ts`: the only allowed skill keys and topic keys.
- `src/content/learning/missions/1-whats-in-spend.json`: the finished template. Match its tone, length, structure and quality.
- `src/content/learning/index.ts`: the exact file names the loader imports. Create exactly these files.
- `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-learning-and-guide-design.md` §4 (learning design, mission table in §4.1, step types, mechanics, authoring rules in §4.4).
- `docs/design.md` §9 (voice) and `docs/domain.md` (glossary: Spend is cash the child holds; Save is money the parent keeps for the child; transfers between jars are not income; spending from Spend doesn't change a savings estimate when saving is unchanged).
- `REQUIREMENTS.md` FR06, FR08, FR10.

## Files to create
1. `src/content/learning/missions/2-small-buys-add-up.json` — key `small-buys-add-up`, day 2, title "Small buys add up", topic `spending`, skill `balance.running_total`.
2. `src/content/learning/missions/3-spend-and-save.json` — key `spend-and-save`, day 3, title "Spend and Save", topic `splitting`, skills `split.amounts` and `split.share`. Must include at least two `split` steps (the slider). Remember `total` and every rule value must be multiples of `increment`.
3. `src/content/learning/missions/4-how-many-weeks.json` — key `how-many-weeks`, day 4, title "How many weeks?", topic `saving-goal`, skills `goal.weeks_needed` (always round UP: 30.00 at 4.00 a week is 8 weeks, not 7.5; use this as a misconception) and `goal.progress` (saved so far and what's left; only Save counts, due allowances don't).
4. `src/content/learning/missions/5-this-or-that.json` — key `this-or-that`, day 5, title "This or that?", topic `tradeoffs`, skills `tradeoff.give_up` (choosing one thing means giving up another) and `tradeoff.need_want` (a `sort` step for needs and wants works well).
5. `src/content/learning/missions/6-plans-change.json` — key `plans-change`, day 6, title "Plans change", topic `changing-plans`, skill `plan.adjust` (a price goes up, a weekly amount changes, a gift arrives, a week is skipped: what happens to the plan and how to adjust it calmly).
6. `src/content/learning/missions/7-put-it-together.json` — key `put-it-together`, day 7, title "Put it together", topic `mixed`, 8 steps using several different skills in NEW situations (not copies of earlier steps).
7. `src/content/learning/topics/{spending,splitting,saving-goal,tradeoffs,changing-plans}.json` — one per topic (FR08: an explanation, a worked example and a chance to practise). `key` is the topic key, `title` is a short friendly title, `intro` one sentence, `explanation` 2 to 4 short paragraphs, `example` with a title and 2 to 5 lines, `practice` one answerable step (with a `key`, no replay needed), `skills` lists that topic's skill keys from skills.ts.
8. `src/content/learning/reviews/{spending,splitting,saving-goal,tradeoffs,changing-plans}.json` — each a JSON array of answerable steps (no `info`, no `replay`), at least 4 items for EVERY skill of that topic (so 9 skills × 4 = at least 36 items overall). These are spaced-review questions that use unfamiliar situations (FR06 "different situations", FR10 "unfamiliar examples"), so do not reuse mission wording or numbers. Keys must be unique across ALL five review files (prefix them, e.g. `afford-r1`, `running-r3`, `share-r2`).
9. `src/content/learning/check-in.json` — `{ "version": 1, "questions": [...] }` with exactly 5 answerable steps, one per topic, in topic order, simple (choice or amount). Copy for the screen is handled by the app; just write the questions.

## Content rules (from the spec, binding)
- Each mission: 6 to 8 steps, an `info` step first with a worked example, at least 4 answerable steps, a mix of step types across the journey (choice, amount, predict, reason, split, sort, match). Every mission has at least one `predict` or `reason` step. The `predict` type needs an `outcome` explaining what happens.
- Give a `replay` (same type, same skill, different numbers or situation) to every `amount`, `split` and number-based `choice` step in missions. Sort, match and reason steps may skip replay.
- Amounts are fictional, written as `{m:NNN}` in minor units (`{m:1250}` shows as £12.50 in a GBP family). Use amounts between {m:50} and {m:10000}, mostly multiples of 50. Two-digit arithmetic at most: the child should be able to do every sum in their head.
- Reading age about 10. At most 25 words per prompt. Plain words, second person, short sentences.
- Feedback voice: correct answers say things like "Nice. That's it." or "Right." followed by why. Wrong answers start "Not quite." and explain the likely mix-up. Never "Incorrect", never "oops", no exclamation marks in corrections, no shame words, never call an amount small or the child poor. Every option's `feedback` must make sense if the child picked it.
- Hints: two per answerable step, the first gentle, the second stronger but not the answer itself.
- No brand names, no shops, no real prices that will date. A mix of names without gender cues when people appear (Sam, Alex, Jo, Ari, Remi, Kai, Noor, Lee...).
- Do not recommend buying things, investments, crypto or specific products. The app teaches thinking, not shopping.
- Misconceptions in `amount` steps: list the 1 to 3 most likely wrong answers with feedback that names the mix-up (added instead of subtracted, forgot to round up, used the wrong jar...). A misconception value must never equal the answer.
- Keys are kebab-case, at most 40 characters, unique within each mission.

## Validation (no Node)
You must NOT run node, npm, npx, pnpm, tsx, bun, vitest or any build tool, and must not install anything. Validate with python3 only: parse every file with `json.load`, and write a small python3 script in your scratch area that checks the main rules (step counts, unique keys, answers in options, split values multiples of increment and within total, sort items point at existing buckets and every bucket has an item, misconceptions not equal to answers, prompts at most 25 words counting a `{m:...}` token as one word, skill keys from the allowed list, at least 4 review items per skill, unique review keys across files). Fix everything it finds. You may use WebSearch to check that topics line up with the Jump$tart grade-8 personal finance standards or the UK Young Money KS3 framework, but keep searches brief.

## Scope
Create only the files listed above. Do not edit any other file, do not touch git, do not commit. When done, report: the files you wrote, each mission's step types in order, the number of review items per skill, and any judgment calls the product owner should check.

| Mission | Steps after `info` |
| --- | --- |
| 2 Small buys add up | amount, amount, predict, choice, sort, reason |
| 3 Spend and Save | split, amount, split, choice, predict, match |
| 4 How many weeks? | amount, amount, sort, amount, predict, reason |
| 5 This or that? | choice, sort, amount, amount, predict, reason |
| 6 Plans change | amount, predict, amount, choice, sort, reason |
| 7 Put it together (8 steps) | choice (`balance.afford`), amount (`balance.running_total`), split (`split.share`), choice (`goal.progress`), sort (`tradeoff.need_want`), predict (`plan.adjust`), reason (`tradeoff.give_up`) |

Every amount, split and number-based choice step has a replay with the same skill and new numbers or a new situation.

**Review items per skill (47 in total):** 5 each for `balance.afford`, `balance.running_total`, `split.amounts`, `split.share`, `goal.weeks_needed`, `goal.progress`, `tradeoff.give_up` and `tradeoff.need_want`. `plan.adjust` has 7, since it is the only skill in its topic. Every review key has a skill prefix (`afford-r1`, `adjust-r7` and so on) and is unique across all five files.

**Check-in:** five questions in topic order: amount, amount, amount (weeks, rounding up), choice (what you give up), choice (plan change).

**Judgment calls for the product owner:**
1. **Who decides the split.** The split steps let the child choose the Spend/Save split, and mission 6 and its topic page offer "save a bit more" as a fix. In the pilot the parent records the split at handover, so the owner may prefer "plan to save" wording.
2. **Needs and wants depend on context.** Bus fare, a school-trip lunch and sports shoes are filed as needs. Review `needwant-r4` teaches that the same thing can be a need for one person and a want for another.
3. **Weeks questions say "You start from zero"** to remove any doubt about money already saved. Mission 4 lists 26 as a wrong answer for working out £30 − £4. Drop it if it seems unlikely.
4. **Rounding down on purpose.** `afford-r4` (how many tickets you can afford) rounds down, while every goal-weeks question rounds up. The contrast is deliberate.
5. **Spend versus Save.** The predict steps contrast spending from Spend (goal date doesn't move) with taking money out of Save (mission 5: the goal comes 2 weeks later).
6. **Some items share a format with mission steps.** `progress-r1`, `amounts-r2`, `weeks-r2` and the check-in questions reuse mission formats with new names and numbers. If "unfamiliar" should be stricter, rework those first.
7. **Wording:**
   - "tradeoff" is one word, to match `topicTitles`.
   - I kept "cinema" and "T-shirt" (mission 1 already uses "Cinema ticket").
   - I swapped the UK-only "torch", "jigsaw" and "PE" for "kite", "model kit" and "sports shoes for school".
   - `{m:50}` will show as "£0.50 to spare".
8. **Two flagged words that stay.**
   - "Small" appears only in the given mission 2 title.
   - My checker flags one option, "Because wants are bad". That is a deliberate wrong answer, and its feedback says wants are fine.

My one standards search confirmed the grade-8 anchors the spec cites: 8-1 budgeting toward goals, 8-2c what makes saving hard, 8-2d how saving decisions affect well-being. Sources: [KSDE summary of the national standards](https://www.ksde.gov/Portals/0/CSAS/CSAS%20Home/Financial%20Literacy/Summary%20of%20National%20Standards%20for%20Personal%20Financial%20Education.pdf), [Real Money Real World alignment](https://realmoneyrealworld.osu.edu/schools/alignment-national-standards).

**PR #28 review (feat/learning-journey vs origin/main)**

I found one blocking bug, seven things to fix and some nits. I scanned the diff for obfuscated code first and found none. I ran nothing except git, grep and `gh pr checks`. The e2e check is still pending in CI.

**Blocking**

1. `src/features/learning/server/actions.ts:45` with `src/app/(app)/child/learn/check-in/page.tsx:17`: after the check-in, the child never sees their result. `completeCheckInAction` calls `refresh()`, which re-renders the page (confirmed in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/.../refresh.md`). `CheckInContent` then sees `overview.checkIn` and returns the "Check-in done." section instead of `<MissionPlayer>`. The player is removed, so "Thanks… You already knew N of 5" is never shown, and `tests/e2e/learning.spec.ts:124` should fail.
   - Fix: drop `refresh()` here, since Learn and Home are request-time and refetch on navigation. Or keep the player mounted after it finishes.

**Should fix**

2. `mission-player.tsx:111,148,165,212,225`: a dropped connection crashes the page. `createAction` only turns server-side errors into results, so a network failure rejects the call. Inside `startTransition` that goes to `src/app/error.tsx` and replaces the whole page. So `SAVE_FAILED` ("Check your connection") and the "Try again" buttons never appear for the case they were written for.
   - Fix: wrap each action call in try/catch, then `setError(SAVE_FAILED)` and return false.
3. `server/service.ts:71` with `components/learning-summary.tsx:61`: replayed missions show the wrong date. Each completed attempt takes the mission's *latest* `completedOn` from the journey. Replaying a done mission (the path offers "You can play it again") gives two rows with the same date and a duplicate React key.
   - Fix: use `completedOn: localDateOf(attempt.completedAt, timezone)`, add `attemptId` to each row and use it as the key.
4. Focus problems in `mission-player.tsx`:
   - Line 487: the topic page player has no intro, so `useFocusOnMount` focuses the question on load and scrolls past the explanation. Only move focus after a user action, not on first mount.
   - Line 287: the done heading has `tabIndex={-1}` but nothing focuses it, so focus is lost when Continue disappears. Add a ref and focus it when the stage changes.
   - Line 639: "Try again" removes itself, so focus is lost. Focus the prompt heading or the first input.
   - Line 583: the `role="status"` box appears together with its text, so screen readers may not announce it. Focus lands on the h3 only, so the explanation text isn't read. Keep a live region that is always there and render the feedback inside it, or focus the whole feedback box.
5. `components/step-inputs.tsx:178,192`: keyboard users can't see which sort answer is focused. The radios are `sr-only` and the labels have no focus style. Add `has-focus-visible:ring-2 has-focus-visible:ring-ring` to the label.
6. `src/features/learning/content.ts:1`: the file has no `import "server-only"`, so only a code comment keeps content out of client bundles. Add the import; `tests/setup.ts:5` already stubs it for tests.
7. The player's step-by-step flow has no component test. Add a Testing Library test with mocked actions covering:
   - resuming in the replay phase;
   - resuming with every step answered, which should call `completeMissionAction`;
   - replays being added only once;
   - a failed save followed by a successful retry.

**Nit**

8. `mission-player.tsx:118-122`: if `startMission` returns an open attempt with every step answered (for example from a second tab), the queue is empty and line 332 renders a blank screen. Separately, line 164 (`if (!attemptId) return;`) leaves "Saving your answers…" on screen with no error or retry. Fix: pass the attempt id into `finish` instead of reading it from state, and call `finish` when the queue is empty.
9. `journey.ts:106`: the fallback `next === -1 ? 0 : next` restarts the mission from step 0 when an earlier step has no result. Return the first unanswered step instead.
10. `server/actions.ts:20-24`: the comment says practice refreshes, but nothing does. Lines 38-41 are a needless wrapper; use `createAction(recordPracticeSchema, recordPractice)`.
11. `server/service.ts:105-109,123`: the whole overview (six queries) is built just to check the lock, then `listAttempts` runs again. Check the lock from the attempts alone.
12. `mission-player.tsx:616`: the "Read about this topic" link is smaller than the 44 px target. Add `inline-flex min-h-11 items-center`.
13. `src/features/allowance/schemas.ts:16`: the re-export means `@/features/allowance` now also exports the date helpers. Point its three internal callers at `@/lib` and remove it.
14. Labels: `mission-player.tsx:385` says "Mission progress" in practice, check-in and topic mode. `components/path-map.tsx:22` says "Your seven-day journey" on the parent's page.
15. `src/app/(app)/child/page.tsx` and `src/app/(app)/parent/children/[childId]/page.tsx`: `getLearningOverview` sits in the same `Promise.all` as the money data, so a learning query error blanks the whole page. Give the learning card its own Suspense section.

**Checked, nothing found**
- **Barrels and imports:** no deep imports from other modules. The client barrel exports nothing server-only. `server/index.ts` starts with `import "server-only"`. Client files only reach `"use server"` actions.
- **Layering:** table and column names appear only in `repository.ts`.
- **Auth:** every service entry calls `assertRole` or `assertSelfOrParentOf`, and pages call `requireRole` or `loadChild`. No `getSession()`.
- **Cache Components:** every session or params read is inside `<Suspense>`, and `ChildNav` is now in one too.
- **Actions:** all five use `createAction` with Zod schemas, and errors are `AppError`s.
- **Banned APIs:** no `console` and no `process.env` in `src`.
- **Next 16 APIs:** `PageProps`, Promise params/searchParams and `refresh` are used correctly.
- **Content:** it reaches the client only as the one mission or set each page passes in, which is the intended design.
- **`journey.ts`:** `chooseNextStep` follows the FR12 order.

**Verdict:** not mergeable yet. Fix #1, which likely fails e2e, and #2–#3. The rest are small.

The accepted risk (a child can forge their own learning stats by calling the functions directly) is documented in ADR 0009; don't re-report it unless it reaches beyond their own stats.

I found nothing Critical or High in PR #28. There is one Medium and one Low. I reviewed commit 128f4b0. The working tree is currently on `feat/money-guide` with someone else's uncommitted changes, so I checked that every file I read is identical to `feat/learning-journey`. The obfuscation scan was clean: no lines over 500 characters, no eval, no child_process.

**Critical:** none.

**High:** none.

**Medium: children can write unlimited rows by calling the functions directly**
- Where: /Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261030100000_learning_progress.sql:205-229 (`start_mission`), :281-287 (`record_step_result`), :393-399 (`record_practice_result`).
- Attack: a child can call these functions straight from the browser with their own session token and the public key. The SQL accepts any mission, step or item name that matches the pattern, so a simple loop can insert rows (up to about 2 KB each) without limit. It can:
  - call `start_mission` with new names, or switch between versions 1 and 2 (each switch abandons one attempt and inserts another);
  - send `record_step_result` with new step names;
  - send `record_practice_result` with new item names.
- Why it goes beyond ADR 0009: the database is shared, so every family pays in storage and cost. It also breaks pages: `listAttempts` and `listStepResults` load everything with no paging, and `.in("attempt_id", …)` (/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/learning/server/repository.ts:100-104) gets a huge id list. That would make the child's Home and Learn pages and the parent's learning page fail.
- Fix: add per-child limits inside the functions and raise `JA429` when they are hit. For example: at most 20 step results per attempt, and at most about 20 attempts and 50 practice results per family day. Alternatively, check names against a small table filled from the content files. Add pgTAP tests for the limits.

**Low: answers stored in the database are never read, and can hold free text**
- Where: same migration :43, :89, :104 (`answer` and `answers` jsonb columns); checks at :274, :386, :452 only test the type and size.
- Problem: the app never reads these columns (the repository's selects at :102, :165, :203 leave them out). Called directly, the functions accept any JSON object, so a child could store free text, including personal details, of up to 2 KB per row and 8 KB per check-in. Their parents could then read it through the API. For an app aimed at children that is extra data with no use.
- Fix: drop the columns, or restrict them in SQL to the five answer kinds with only option ids or integers as values.

**Areas with nothing to report:**
- **Authorization:** every server action calls `assertRole("child")` first.
  - `recordStep` and `completeMission` check the attempt belongs to the child twice: once in the app and again in the database (`child_id <> v_uid` raises JA403).
  - Locked missions cannot be started (`canOpenMission`).
  - The parent page checks the child is in the parent's family (`loadChild`), and `getLearningOverview` repeats the check (`assertSelfOrParentOf`).
  - Read access in the database requires both parent and child to be approved family members. A profile can only be in one family.
  - The database tests cover brothers or sisters, parents from another family, a child in no family, and signed-out users.
- **Grants:**
  - The internal helper `learning_child_family()` is revoked from every role, including `service_role`.
  - The five write functions are revoked from `public` and `anon` and granted only to `authenticated`.
  - `service_role` keeps its default access, but it gets JA401 because it has no signed-in user.
  - Every function sets `search_path = ''` and checks all its arguments.
  - The advisory lock uses its own `learning:` prefix, separate from the money `ledger:` locks.
  - Repeat calls are safe (`on conflict do nothing`), and locking the attempt row stops record and complete from running at the same time.
  - Signed-in users cannot insert, update, delete or truncate the tables directly.
- **Input validation:** the Zod schemas in `/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/features/learning/schemas.ts` are strict. Answers contain no free text, maps are capped at 8 entries, and the key pattern rules out `__proto__`.
- **`?from=`:** the value is only used after it is matched to a real mission (`findMission`), so only a known mission name reaches the link. The sign-in return path always starts with `/child/learn/` and goes through `safeNextPath`. No open redirect and no injection.
- **Leaks to the client:** the client import file does not export the learning content, and the repository never sends answers or other children's rows. Nothing uses `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; `renderText` returns plain strings. Answer keys reaching the browser is an accepted risk in ADR 0009. One wording mismatch: the ADR says answers ship "with the active step", but the mission page passes the whole mission, including replays (`/Users/harisshakeel/Developer/Jarso/src/app/(app)/child/learn/[missionKey]/page.tsx:38`). The content is already public in the repo, so only the ADR wording needs a fix.
- **Logging:** the learning feature does no logging of its own. `toAppError` logs only the error code and message.
- **Secrets and personal data:** the diff contains only placeholders (`sk-ant-…` in the spec) and `example.com` test accounts. The end-to-end test reads the local Supabase secret key into memory and never prints it.

timestamp: 2026-10-29T20:36:56.713Z
